# Ciaobot Architecture

### Curation state integrity

The nightly curation state is strict v1: only a genuinely absent file initializes as empty. Malformed, unreadable, duplicate-key, partial, or unsupported state raises `CurationStateError` and remains byte-for-byte untouched. Care and lease-dependent attended actions refuse uncertainty; recovery requires restoring a known-good state file, with no automatic reset or repair. A missing file cannot prove prior state was not deleted, and validation cannot fence non-cooperating edits after the validation seam.

The packaged decision logs have one writer boundary (#3101, A1, macOS and Linux only): `ciao curation-log-append` and the weekly marker both run under the canonical state lock, so a committed append is not lost to a marker rewrite. Both are checked by real two-process contention in `tests/test_curation_log_portability.py`. Oversized logs (over 64 KB) are blocked, not rotated: `curation-begin` reports them under `deferred` with `blocked_count` and they take no runnable budget. Activation requires operator-verified quiescence of every older writer, binary and cached prompt, and this change does not establish that. Windows is not verified.

Orientation doc for the codebase. Read this before making any change in `ciao/`, `web/`, `scripts/`, or `deploy/`. After a change that affects layout, capabilities, env vars, endpoints, or commands, update this file, `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md`, and `INTEGRATIONS.md` so they match the repo.

## A meta-harness for personal AI assistance

Ciaobot is a **meta-harness**: a persistent personal workspace around existing agent harnesses, not a replacement model/tool execution loop. The layers are:

1. **Ciaobot — context and continuity.** Workspace and project context, chats and transcripts, files and feedback, automations, memory extraction and review, and scope-enforcing application operations.
2. **Agent harnesses — execution.** Claude Code through `claude-agent-acp` (ACP), and OpenCode through `opencode acp` (2.3.2+), handle model interaction and tool execution using the user's configured provider access. Ciaobot starts and supervises these sessions through provider adapters.
3. **Models and tools.** The configured cloud or local models, filesystem access, and integrations reached through each harness.

The product is organised around one person's work and knowledge, not an autonomous company's org chart. Its continuity loop is **request → run → output → durable knowledge**: project context accompanies work, archived conversations can produce scoped Markdown facts, and uncertain facts wait for review.

Provider-neutral application context and durable Markdown knowledge do not imply provider-session portability or identical tools, permissions, and capabilities. Switching harnesses preserves the personal workspace, not every detail of the execution environment. Application-operation guards are not a sandbox for arbitrary agent filesystem or credential access.

For task-oriented reading paths, start with the [documentation hub](README.md).

The Chat details rail is a closable docked window, using the pinned file viewer's visual vocabulary. `AgentContextSection.vue` shares readable project context and the canonical-document link between the rail and narrow-pane drawer; the underlying capsule and its token estimate are unchanged.
Chat file links resolve an exact canonical identity via `/api/chats/{chat_id}/file-path`, using the same agent root the provider executes in. Mentions in a completed reply that were never touched by a tool call are discovered by the separate browser-only `POST /api/chats/{chat_id}/file-paths/resolve` (#2118), which confines itself to the owning workspace's roots (see the filesystem-scope section). Manual pin requests use that root too. Once resolved, text, image, binary and HTML viewers all consume the absolute identity; pin failures never dismiss the preview (#1167).

Chat references in Markdown use the same active-project, local, unarchived chat set as the `@`-mention picker. `ChatPanel.vue` derives mention choices once and uses those rows for both the picker and the chat-link map, so a reference never links a conversation the reader cannot open from the offered list. The map key includes only chat id/title pairs; activity-only chat-list polls preserve its `Map` identity and leave both Markdown caches intact.
The task board derives four status groups and draws them as lanes side by side, each a fixed 310px wide at every pane width, with the Done lane collapsed behind a tab at the end of the row (`ciao.tasks.doneShown` in localStorage). A narrow pane scrolls the lanes sideways inside the board element, so the page never scrolls sideways; drag and Shift+Left/Right do not depend on pane width. The manual order of each column is one id sequence per status in `<vault>/Workspace/task-order.json` (`ciao/task_order.py`, see the layout below). Reads reconcile that file against the task files and never write it; a reorder or a status change with a position carries the order version the client read (`expected_order_version`), and a stale one is a 409 `task_order_conflict` that writes nothing. A status change with a placement writes the order file under the workspace lock before the task file and restores it if the task write fails. The web store adopts only the `order` the server returns and rolls back a failed write, so the client never sorts tasks itself. Cards, lanes and sidebar projects share one drag-sort engine, `web/src/composables/useDragSort.ts`, whose pure geometry is in `web/src/lib/dragSort.ts`; rows drag by their own surface with no handle, and reorder by the row menu's Move up / Move down. A card's Move… sheet chooses a column and landing row for a cross-lane placement, one write through `place()`. The engine persists nothing and hands each finished move to a commit callback; `useMenuFocusReturn` gives the trigger's focus back after a menu action without stealing it once the user has focused or pressed elsewhere. Done is one gesture: the completion sheet has one primary action and an optional note, and sends both on the same POST as the status — blank completes without a resolution, nonblank text is kept as typed. The Chat details rail's task origin and the archive-chat dialog follow the same contract: one completion action, and the note is optional in the field itself.

Production `/assets` serves hashed JavaScript and CSS with gzip negotiation above a 500-byte minimum; API responses and chat streams are outside that compression path. `ChatLayout.vue` loads chat and pinned-file panels on demand, begins the chat download for chat deep links alongside bootstrap data, and loads the file viewer on first open while retaining its mounted dialog lifecycle. Startup progress and the engine monitor share a bounded status request, and a short initial display delay avoids a ready-state curtain flash without persisting readiness. The monitor does not read one failed probe as a dead engine. The PWA is served to phones and laptops that reach the engine over a VPN or a relay, where a request that a loopback origin answers in milliseconds can take seconds, so the status request gets a ten-second budget, a request that merely runs out of time is graded as weak evidence and only becomes an outage after the engine has gone unanswered for twenty seconds, and the awareness socket — which the engine pushes a frame on every few seconds — is the arbiter: while it is still delivering, a failed probe is the path being slow and the "isn't reachable" curtain stays down. A request that is refused or errored is still decisive, so a crash is still reported within seconds.

Chat listings batch cross-cwd Claude session availability checks into one fresh
directory scan per request. Individual session lookups retain their separate
freshness contract; listing availability is never cached across requests.

Settings → MCP is one button per installed agent. Each button starts that agent's CLI (`mcp_command` on its provider row: `claude`, `opencode` or `codex`) in the in-app terminal for the active workspace's project, then types the row's `mcp_slash` (`/mcp`, or `/mcps` for OpenCode) once the CLI has started. The page does not read MCP inventory or turn servers on or off. Changes apply to new chats; a running chat loads them after `ciao chat reload`. A CLI that is not installed has no button.

## App repo layout

```
README.md                      Product intro, quickstart, doc index.
AGENTS.md                      Contributor guide for coding agents. Native providers load it at session start (Claude Code reads it directly since 2.1.277).
INTEGRATIONS.md                Operator config (env vars, OAuth, MCP connectors, server runtime knobs).
PWA_API.md                     PWA API reference (endpoints, auth flow, state path).
CONTRIBUTING.md                Contribution guide.
docs/                          Long-form docs (this file, DEVELOPMENT.md).

For nightly curation, category cluster materialization is explicit in `curation-begin`; the canonical worklist builder consumes prepared items only. `curation-plan` and `curation-end` omit category generation and report incomplete observed counts because discovery is not exhaustive. Begin's category generation is split into a read-only vault scan (outside every lock) and a publication step that takes the proposal queue lock, then the curation state lock, re-checks the exact live holder, and only then writes the sidecar and queue bullet; a lost lease publishes nothing and exits 75. Successful end still records run state and may advance the weekly marker.

ciao/                          Python backend (Starlette).
  tool_summary.py              Unchanged provider-specific tool summaries and OpenCode file-touch projection shared by live and replay consumers.
  main.py                      Web server entry point, route wiring, startup hooks.
  _project_vocabulary.py       Isolated deterministic #3623 verifier for one project-vocabulary sentence: whole-message bytes in, one of three fixed frames parsed against disjoint finite slot dictionaries (optional cadence + planning/review + meeting; optional weekday + huddle/sync), approved literal compounds matched atomically, literal terms plus fixed rendering, exact byte spans/digest; unknown words, added clauses, multiple parses, mixed text, negation, quotation/forwarding, conditions, qualifiers, uncertainty/temporal/correction, code/instructions, secrets, private identifiers and sensitive/mixed content defer privately (explicit detectors, not a claim of complete privacy or truth detection); stdlib-only, no I/O; ordinary chat ingress, standing consent/grants, evidence, producer and publication integrations remain unwired.
  cli.py                       Packaged `ciao` CLI: run, supervise, setup, setup-url, dev, auth, scaffold, sync-skills, skills-sync, skills, vault-index, vault-search, vault-lint, os-audit, memory-audit, label-hygiene, cleanup-sdk-blobs, create-chat, eval, public-preflight, package-smoke, prepare-release, health, and desktop-service commands. Its launchd renderer is the service consumer B2 built (`_render_launchd_plist`/`_write_launchd_plist` accept a verified `HostOwnership` and render the exact `host_service_argv` + `ExitTimeOut` 45), and `setup_workspace`/`_register_launchd_service` are the E2 activation point: `_verified_host_if_installed` selects the hosted service only from `ciao.server_host.verify_owned_host`, `_host_engine_interpreter` maps the installer's `--python $ciao` console to the engine interpreter the host must serve, `_existing_hosted_definition_serves` keeps an already-hosted definition whose named host executable still exists from being downgraded (a dead definition falls through to the direct shape so the repair path works), and a render-time refusal becomes a `RuntimeError` message, never a traceback (#1060).
  __main__.py                   `python -m ciao`: the same entry point as the `ciao` console script, delegating to `ciao.cli.main` (#791).
  setup_status.py              Bootstrap/setup readiness API and wizard finish handler.
  onboarding.py                Owner-private atomic lifecycle receipt for resumable onboarding; distinct from the setup provisioning marker and welcome-pending flag. Strictly validates schema and global revision, initializes existing installs as legacy-exempt, and rejects corrupt state rather than resetting it.
  onboarding_progress.py       Transport-neutral progress operations over the receipt; reads never initialize, new workspace creation registers runtime progress, and rename moves identity using the receipt writer's global revision contract.
  dev.py                       Local dev runner: backend on :8543 plus Vite frontend on :5173. CLI: `ciao dev`.
  config.py                    Env var loading, workspace config.
  opencode_cli.py              OpenCode CLI helpers outside a chat: the binary, the Settings status and credential inventory, the skill list, and the version floor (`cli_version_error`, 2.3.2+) shared with the ACP start gate, the releaser and the conversation importer.
  claude_trim.py               Catalog of Claude harness trim options (tool denies, bundled-skill overrides, disable* settings) with defaults, and the helpers that turn a workspace's sparse claude_trim map into denies and the CLI settings layer.
  port_in_use.py               The engine's port is taken by something else (#1654). One place owns the wording every reader shares: the operator-facing sentence (which port, and the two ways out — free it, or set `PWA_PORT` in the workspace `.env`), the port-in-use exit code (69: not the restart code 75, the workspace-access code 78 or the setup-memory code 3, so a service manager reads it as a failed run and not as "relaunch me"), the startup phase the failure is recorded under (`server_starting`), the errno test (`EADDRINUSE`), the bind the engine makes itself (`check_server_port`, with the addresses and the socket options asyncio's own `create_server` would use, so a failure here is the failure uvicorn would have had) and the probe the installer's preflight uses (`port_is_available`, the same bind, so its answer is the engine's). The reason is also *recorded*, outside every workspace at `~/.local/state/ciaobot/port-in-use.txt` (the way `workspace_access` records its denial), because an engine that cannot bind cannot serve the endpoint that would report it: the reader has to find the reason somewhere else. `ciao.main` binds the port itself (`check_server_port`), records the failed phase, and leaves with the port-in-use code; `ciao.supervise` holds the job on that code instead of exiting, so the service manager does not respawn the engine into the same failure.
  providers/                   Claude Code, Codex and OpenCode over ACP (`acp/`), the shared base, and one-shot calls.
    stdio_rpc.py               Async JSON-lines RPC process transport used by CLI app-server adapters.
    acp/agents.py              The real ACP agent specs (`CLAUDE`, `CODEX`, `OPENCODE`) and the provider classes that run them.
    oneshot.py                 Single-turn provider call (max_turns=1, no tools). Used by routine-model automations.
    acp/spec.py                `AgentSpec` and `ModeChoice`: per-agent data (binary, argv, fixed env, `clientCapabilities._meta` opt-ins, the config options each Ciaobot mode maps to, quiet period, initialize timeout) for the ACP client layer (#3137). No code outside this data names an agent.
    acp/connection.py          `AcpConnection`: spawns one ACP agent from an `AgentSpec` (argv from the spec only, engine environment minus Ciaobot's own internals: `CIAO_*` and the PWA token are withheld, while provider credentials pass through and the CLI handles its own login; in the bundled Mac engine `SSL_CERT_FILE` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` are dropped too, see `os_support/child_env.py`; the spec's `unset_env` keys are kept out; engine bin dir first on PATH; a relative resolved binary is refused), connects the Agent Client Protocol SDK to its stdio and runs `initialize` with raw `_meta` capabilities. Keeps a bounded stderr ring (`StderrRing`), raises `AgentStartError` with the stderr tail when the binary is missing, the process exits or `initialize` times out, and `close()` is idempotent and cancellation-safe: the agent stays in the engine's process group (so a group kill still reaches it) and its whole descendant tree, found by a ppid walk (`os_support/processes.py` `process_table`/`descendant_processes`), gets SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5 s. The in-app terminal filters its environment on purpose differently: it strips secrets (`*_TOKEN`, `*_API_KEY`, `GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`, and similar), while agent processes inherit the engine environment minus `CIAO_*` and `PWA_AUTH_TOKEN`. `connect_client` routes each spec's unprefixed `extension_notifications` (opencode's `child_update`, codex's `auth/status_update`) to `ext_notification`, and drops any other unknown notification at DEBUG (once per method) so the SDK never logs it as an unhandled ERROR; requests still get method-not-found.
    acp/__init__.py            Agent Client Protocol client layer package (#3137).
    acp/normalize.py           Pure ACP `session/update` -> StreamEvent translation: key canonicalisation, tool-call tracking by id (refresh, result), subagent parent ids (Claude `parentToolUseId`, opencode child-session meta), usage, model/mode config values, compaction flag, per-turn top-level text (`TurnText`) and the stop-reason -> `ResultEvent` mapping. No I/O.
    acp/shell_liveness.py      Background shell liveness: the pid and start time a launch acknowledgement names, checked against the process table every few seconds (`ps`, never a signal); a pid that vanished or whose start time changed settles the call and emits one `ShellFinishedEvent` (the call id is remembered, so a replayed acknowledgement never re-fires). Pure, the table is passed in.
    acp/shell_output.py        The output tail of a finished shell, read only from the path its launch metadata reports (`metadata.outputPath`; no provider sends one yet, so tails are empty until one does). Allowed: no `.`/`..` segment, realpath equal to the path (no symlinked parent), under the OpenCode shell root (`<XDG data>/opencode/shell`) or with Claude's `tasks/<id>.output` shape (Claude's root is not determined here, so only the shape and realpath apply). A regular, non-blocking open (a FIFO is refused), owned by the engine's user, the last 64 KiB at most, sanitised by `normalize.sanitize_output` (every category-C character, U+2028/2029, unterminated OSC), 20 lines and 2000 bytes at most; an oversized last line keeps its end. `None` when unreadable, `[]` when empty.
    acp/permissions.py         ACP permission broker: host-generated request IDs stay unique even when a provider reuses a tool-call id; boolean cards require one unambiguous `allow_once` and select only unique `allow_once` / `reject_once` choices (otherwise `cancelled`). Shell cards carry the exact bounded command, invalid or unattended requests never show a card, and cancel answers every pending request. `mode_choices` / `pick_value` pick the agent config value for a Ciaobot mode.
    acp/session.py             One ACP session (`AcpSession`): open via `session/load`, else `session/resume`, else `session/new` (`_meta` on all three; additional directories only when advertised; a failed or unsupported resume is replaced), one prompt at a time, config apply, late turns after background updates, cancel, and a private 0600 JSONL journal (`SessionJournal`) under `<runtime>/acp-journal`.
    acp/provider.py            `AcpProvider`: the `BaseProvider` for any ACP agent (a subclass sets `spec`, and may override the `session_meta` / `oneshot_meta` / `spawn_env` hooks). Owns one agent process and at most one live session, reuses the connection on the key (provider reuse key, linked repo, workspace), always opens a fresh session when there is no resume id, reloads a live session when its `session_meta` changes (never under a late turn), maps a start failure to a short sanitized reason (`AgentStartError.stderr_tail` is logged, never sent to the PWA), and carries the `unattended` flag into the permission broker. Registered through `ProviderDescriptor.acp_spec_path`; the `claude` and `codex` descriptors set it (#3138, #3137). OpenCode runs over ACP too (`opencode acp`, `OpencodeAcpProvider` in `acp/agents.py`). A native slash command is sent as two blocks when the spec sets `slash_capsule_as_block`: the bare command, then the context capsule (as an embedded resource when the agent takes them), because claude-agent-acp keeps only the first text block as the command. Persisted-session classmethods (`delete_session`, `read_thread`, `native_title_candidates`) take the explicit `runtime_root` (`config.state_path.parent`), so they address the same journal the live provider wrote even when `CIAO_RUNTIME_ROOT` is outside `<workspace>/.runtime`. `read_thread` returns `{"acp_journal": rows}`, which the transcript service renders into chat history (`_render_acp_journal`). `model_catalog` probes the agent's model and thinking options in a throwaway session (no MCP servers), cached per agent and workspace for 300 s; `force` bypasses the cache, and a failed probe is logged and yields an empty list.
    acp/oneshot.py             `run_acp_oneshot`: one prompt through a throwaway session in a fresh empty temporary directory, in plan mode when the agent offers it, with every permission request rejected (the call is unattended). `ciao/providers/oneshot.py` `run_oneshot` dispatches here for a provider whose descriptor names an `AgentSpec`. An error stop reason raises `OneShotError` with a fixed detail, never the adapter's text.
    acp/agents.py              OpenCode over `opencode acp` (#3140): the `OPENCODE` spec (no bypass mode, every Ciaobot mode maps to `build`, `opencode/child-session-updates` opt-in), its version gate (`VersionGate`, refusing below 2.3.2 at start with the fixed `OPENCODE_V2_REQUIRED` text), and `OpencodeAcpProvider`, which nests a subagent's child session under the subagent's `Agent` tool call (linked by the launch's description and its `metadata.sessionID`) from `opencode/session/child_update`, a notification opencode sends without the underscore that `connection.connect_client` routes by name. A child with no launch behind it keeps a `Subagent` node (`child:<id>`). Only a child's message and thought chunks and tool calls reach the parent; its usage, model, mode and compaction updates are dropped. Claude Code over `claude-agent-acp` (#3138): the `CLAUDE` spec (memory and artifact env disables, the mode table), `ClaudeAcpProvider`, which sends Ciaobot's disable options as `_meta.claudeCode.options` (`disallowedTools`, `settings` as an object, the connectors env) on session/new, load and resume, and the no-tools one-shot options, `spawn_env` (the user's own `claude` as `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECUTABLE`), and `claude_auth_command`. The adapter runs from the machine's own copy at the pin when one is on the PATH, else from the private prefix (`ciao/adapter_install.py`). Codex's `CodexAcpProvider.spawn_env` sets `CODEX_PATH` to the user's `codex`, and its `ext_update` debug-logs `auth/status_update` (state and account count only, no events; the Providers card does not read it yet). `AcpSession.apply` sets the chat's model exactly when the adapter offers it; a bare tier name (`opus`) maps to the offered model with that tier word through `model_tiers.resolve_tier`. Any other value is not guessed at or sent: the adapter keeps its default and the session reports one status line that the model is not offered. Claude's title arrives as an ACP `session_info_update` and is applied as an adapter title; nothing polls for it (see "Chat titles from the agent" below). Codex over `codex-acp` (#3139): the `CODEX` spec (the Codex modes mapped to `read-only`/`workspace-write`/`agent`/`agent-full-access` and `collaboration_mode`), `CodexAcpProvider`, and `codex_auth_command`, which only builds the `codex login` line the Settings terminal runs. Ciaobot never reads or stores Codex credentials.
  context/                     Provider-neutral per-turn context capsule (workspace, project, vault hints, and one bounded task-board recipe line — never a board dump; see the capsule section).
  schedules.py                 Cron-style schedule dispatch.
  schedule_command.py          The optional shell command a schedule runs before its model turn (#1258): run in the workspace with a timeout, no chat when it exits 0 with empty stdout, otherwise the exit code and capped output go ahead of the prompt. Runs in its own process tree (`os_support.processes`), so a timeout kills pipelines and children too and keeps the partial output.
  task_board.py                Portable per-workspace Markdown task records and a revision-safe store (#978, child of #973): the canonical `<vault>/Workspace/Tasks/<id>.md` contract, a strict frontmatter parser, source-preserving patches, revision-checked `delete` and atomic writes. Reached by `/api/tasks*` and `ciao task` through the workspace-scoped service in `control_plane.py`; the board UI is B4. Its two nullable linkage fields (`chat_id`/`attempt_id`) are refused as ordinary patch fields — an agent naming its own chat would be claiming a delegation it never made — and get their own revision-checked door instead: `link()`/`unlink()`, called only by the delegation service, which is the whole of "a task with a turn in flight cannot be completed or reassigned". A task the agent files through the managed create carries an optional `created_by: agent` frontmatter key (absent for user-filed tasks and every task from before the field existed; never patchable), which the decision log records as `task_created_by`.
  task_order.py                The authoritative manual order of the task board (#2086, part of #2079): one JSON file per workspace, `<vault>/Workspace/task-order.json`, holding an id sequence per status and a version. Pure and stdlib-only: the baseline order (due, accent-stripped title, id), reconcile-on-read (stored ids that still fit, then unlisted tasks in baseline order; reads never write), and `apply_placement` (anchor-based insertion on the full sequence with an optimistic `expected_version`). `TaskBoardStore` does the I/O: create, status change and reorder write the file under the workspace lock, order file first, restored if the task write fails; a malformed file never blocks the board and is never rewritten.
  task_log.py                  The delegation log a task carries in its own Markdown body (#1064): one list item per attempt (when, how it ended in the shared label vocabulary, the chat and its archived transcript, the agent's summary) inside a `<!-- ciao:task-log -->` fence at the end of the body. The durable half of the attempt store, which lives in `.runtime/`: it survives a lost runtime directory and travels with the vault. Only the item for the attempt being recorded is rewritten (matched by its `<!-- attempt:<id> -->` mark); `strip_log` removes the section before the description is quoted into a delegation prompt.
  decision_rules.py            Standing review rules distilled from the operator's decision logs (#1745): `build_digest` groups the last 180 days of operator memory-proposal and task decisions (agent-made ones are only counted, except an agent delete or complete that carries the user's reason, which is operator evidence) under closed-vocabulary keys with sanitised, capped excerpts and ready-made citations; `write_managed` lints model-written rule lines (known key, verbatim cite, no markup, 2500-char cap, no unapproved verdict flip) and splices them between `ciao:rules` markers in `Workspace/Memory-Review-Rules.md` / `Workspace/Task-Rules.md` without touching the operator's text; `load_for_prompt` returns the file fenced as evidence for the memory pass and task delegation. CLI: `ciao decisions-digest`, `ciao decision-rules-write`.
  task_decisions.py            The workspace-scoped, append-only log of operator task decisions (#1726): one JSON row per delete, reject (release of a `ready_for_review` attempt), approve and complete (an agent closing a task on the user's say-so), with a reason (optional except on the agent's delete and complete), in `Workspace/Task-Decisions.jsonl` in the vault. It outlives a deleted task file and is read by the weekly pass. `read_decisions_strict` (#3239-a) is the bounded reader the archive pass's task-decision context uses: it reports absent, unreadable, malformed or oversize instead of skipping lines.
  archive_capture.py           The workspace-scoped, append-only log of archive captures (#3239-c1): one JSON line per archive that enqueues a memory pass, in `Workspace/Archive-Captures.jsonl`, holding ids and counts only (the chat and workspace names, the attempt and decision ids the archive saw, and a status per read; never transcript text, reasons, titles or paths). `captured_at` is the engine clock's instant, the first-seen time a later consumer can trust. `read_captures` is the bounded strict reader (8 MiB ceiling, malformed rows counted, same-identity re-archives folded to the first row, a same-identity row with different counts or ids reported as a conflict and its identity left out). `archive_pipeline.py` writes one record per archive that enqueues a memory pass (#3239-c2), and the approved-task enqueue on an already-archived chat writes its `completion` record through `ProjectChatManager.capture_for_enqueue` before the pass (#3239-c3b); nothing reads the file yet. The live file rolls to a dated `Archive-Captures.<UTC-date>.jsonl` sibling once an append would pass half the read ceiling, keeping the newest 4 rotated files, and the reader reads only the live file, so a row that has rolled out no longer counts for the fold.
  task_context.py              The read-only task-decision context an ordinary archive memory pass carries (#3239-a): the decisions recorded against the attempts that ran in the source chat (newest 10, reasons capped at 300 characters, section capped at 4,000), fenced as data and stated complete or incomplete. Reads only this workspace's attempt store and decisions file; nothing is written or counted.
  linked_chats.py              The read-only linked-chat excerpts an archive memory pass carries (#3239-b): the first and last user message of up to three chats linked to the source (its fork parent and the other chats of its tasks' attempts), same workspace only, newest first, each read from its archive file (no-follow, regular file, 1 MiB ceiling) and labelled L1 to L3; an unavailable archive is marked, and the section is fenced apart from the task-decision context.
  task_resolution.py           The completion history a task carries in its own Markdown body (#1152, child of #1115): one list item per managed transition into `done` (when, the resolution text, the attempt it closed) inside a `<!-- ciao:task-completions -->` fence beside the delegation log. A resolution rewording moves only that item's `edited` stamp, never its `completed_at`; `strip_completions` removes the section before the description is quoted into a delegation prompt.
  task_updates.py              Progress notes on the same task file, inside a `<!-- ciao:task-updates -->` fence: one item per milestone or blocker, labeled user or agent. A description save restores the section, so a note is not an edit of the requested work. The delegation prompt quotes unseen notes; the completion memory pass reads the whole section. Newly written items carry `<!-- encoding:1 -->` so literal section-marker entities round-trip without conflation; unmarked saved items retain their original decoding. Editing upgrades only the selected item. Task log, completion history and progress share the list-item splitter in `_task_markdown.py`, while each section owns its marker syntax and rewrite rules.
  task_attempts.py             Durable delegation attempts (#1033, child B5 of #973): one `TaskAttemptStore` per workspace over an owner-private `<runtime>/task-attempts-<workspace>.json`, written through `mkstemp_private` + fsync + one atomic replace under `keyed_lock` plus an advisory sibling lock, following the same discipline as `webhooks.py`. An attempt is `(attempt_id, task_id, task_revision, chat_id, state)` with `state ∈ running | needs_you | failed | interrupted | ready_for_review | stopped`; `LIVE_STATES` (the first, second and fifth) is what holds the task's linkage and what a second `start` returns instead of minting a second attempt, while `RESUMABLE_STATES` (`failed`/`interrupted`/`stopped`) is what `resume` may continue and `SETTLED_STATES` is what history is. **A crash is derived, not remembered**: every record carries the writing process's `owner` token, so a live attempt owned by another process cannot have a turn running in this one and reads `interrupted` — which is how a restart re-derives it with no startup sweep and nothing remembered. `start` is idempotent per task and reports `created`, taking an optional `attempt_id` because the chat is created *before* this store writes and the chat's provenance stamp has to name the attempt it is for — `ProjectChatManager.update_chat` has no `helper` parameter, so a stamp patched in afterwards would raise and be swallowed; `reopen` is the one door from a settled row back to `running`; `bind_revision` re-stamps the revision an engine-owned write (linkage, review badge, log line, migration) left behind, so an attempt's binding follows the engine's own writes and never a user edit. Since #1064 a live attempt also carries the agent's own report (`outcome ∈ done | blocked | needs_input` plus a `summary`, via `report`), which decides how a cleanly ended turn settles: only `done` is `ready_for_review`, anything else waits on the user as `needs_you`. Also holds the two server-owned prompt builders (`build_prompt`, which hands a new attempt the earlier attempts' outcomes, summaries and transcripts, and `build_resume_prompt`) and the `task_delegation_helper` provenance stamp. Reads take no lock — the derivation is a pure function of the document and the next mutation persists it.
  agent_cli.py                 `ciao <noun> <verb>` agent commands (`memory`, `vault`, `file`, `chat`, `project`, `schedule`, `workspace list`, `repo candidates`, `context`, `stats`, `help`): argparse to `(operation, arguments)`, POST to `CIAO_AGENT_URL/<op>` with `CIAO_AGENT_TOKEN`, one JSON envelope on stdout, exit 0/1/2. `is_agent_invocation` decides which argv `ciao.cli.main` hands over before the operator parser is built (`ciao run` stays the server launcher).
  agent_surface.py             Agent CLI transport behind `POST /agent/v1/{op}` (route in ciao/web/routes_agent.py): verifies the bearer token with the agent session registry, then runs the shared operation function inside the same auth context, so guards, envelope, validation and telemetry are shared; records `surface: "cli"`.
  shell_wake.py                The chat notice for background shells that finished: one wake per 3 s window, built with `format_finished_runs` (origin `background_run`, so the PWA's background-run card draws it). The command loses its leading environment assignments (quoted values too) and goes through `redact_text`; the exit code is shown when the provider sent one, "exit status unknown" otherwise. Wake limit: 6 per chat per 10 min, and no wake once the last three turns were all shell wakes with no person in between; past either limit one capped notice (`paused_prompt`) is sent and the rest dropped until the person replies. A question or permission pending holds the notices until it resolves.
  background_notice.py         The "[Ciaobot] N background runs finished" notice text (`format_finished_runs`) for finished shells, in the shape the PWA's `parseInjectedPrompt` reads.
  dag.py                       Tiny DAG runner (Node kinds: bash / prompt / gate / subagent; edges: ok / fail / always). Subprocess nodes can merge per-node env overrides for routed models. Subagent nodes accept an opt-in `requires` post-condition list (file paths, optionally with a `contains` line regex): exit 0 without the required files is a node failure, not a silent success. Each node is timed via `job_runs.track_sync`, so Automation page shows per-node status. No shipped producer runs one; workspace-owned DAG workflows are its callers.
  sessions.py                  Session state, auth, signed cookies, JSON-backed StateStore for `.runtime/state.json`.
  jsonio.py                    Small JSON I/O helpers.
  claude_sessions.py           SDK-free reader and file delete for Claude Code's session JSONL (ported from claude-agent-sdk 0.2.178, #3138 C2a). Chat history for chats created before the ACP switch reads through it; the transcript path and the Claude ACP provider's session-file delete use it. Claude Code's JSONL layout is not a public contract.
  transcripts.py               Provider-neutral live transcripts and archives under each workspace's `<agent root>/Logs/Chats/` (`config.chats_root`; the shared `<install>/Logs` keeps pre-split and unattributed archives). Each archived turn also records the skills it used (`turn_skills`), and `read_archive_skills` reads that line back with the `## Turn N` anchor it sits under. It is the only evidence a skill-improvement proposal has: a turn's rendered blocks are the user's and the agent's prose, and a skill enters a conversation as a tool call (Claude's `Skill`, opencode's `skill`, or a read of the skill's own `SKILL.md`) or a `<command-name>` marker, none of which was in the archive before. Written and read in the same module so the two shapes cannot drift.
  usage_stats.py               Read-only usage statistics for one workspace (`GET /api/stats`, `ciao stats`): chats opened, typed user messages (by day, hour and project), chats archived, tokens and models per transcript turn, memory decisions and pending proposals, applied `note_apply` receipts, task completions and `schedule_dispatch` job runs, all read from the stores that already record them. `snapshot_sources` copies the chat-registry facts on the event loop; `compute_usage_stats` reads files in a worker thread, caches each completed local day in memory per `(workspace, date)` and recomputes today.
  upgrade.py                   Packaged skill install/update helpers used at boot. Production engine updates are the Mac app's or the Linux installer's, not this module. External-package installs (LibreOffice) are deliberately not automated here: the app surfaces status and an install-prompt chat, never a brew/npm call (issue #359).
  app_engine.py                 Ciaobot-for-Mac app-engine mode (`ciao run --app-engine`, refused outside the app bundle; a hidden `--port N`, 1024-65535, overrides the port for that process and so the ready record's; before setup the app engine binds 127.0.0.1 only, `main._pin_app_engine_bootstrap_host`). `state_dir()` (`~/Library/Application Support/Ciaobot/engine`, 0700) holds the engine's app-owned records, all 0600 and written atomically: `workspace.json` is the workspace pointer (`read_pointer`/`write_pointer`/`clear_pointer`; fail-safe read — absent, corrupt, wrong-schema, relative or non-directory reads as "no pointer", so bootstrap — and clear removes one only while it still names the expected workspace, since a pointer naming another workspace is what stands between this engine and that install), and `ready.json` is the readiness record written under the lock on every start (pid, configured HTTP port, startup_id, workspace, bootstrap). The app-owned engine also serves the same ASGI app on a private IPv4 loopback HTTPS listener. It loads a fresh TLS key through anonymous pipes and sends the bound port and SHA-256 leaf pin through the app's inherited readiness pipe. The ready record remembers the HTTPS port across launches to keep the PWA origin stable. If it is occupied, startup fails and leaves the record intact. The HTTPS listener adds an exact-origin Content Security Policy for network connections and shares the HTTP listener's lifespan and shutdown. `watch_parent` runs a daemon kqueue (`EVFILT_PROC`/`NOTE_EXIT`) thread that SIGTERMs the engine when the app dies; a parent already gone (pid 1, or `ESRCH` at registration) calls back at once. `installed_workspace_env` consults the pointer (never the LaunchAgent plist) in bundled mode, and `setup_workspace` under the app writes the pointer instead of a service definition, so the app mode plants no LaunchAgent. The module imports on every platform even though every behaviour is macOS.
  app_settings.py              Runtime-mutable app settings (per-provider default model/thinking/permission-mode and Session insights models, and the server settings that used to be `.env` variables: bind address, log level, developer mode, source checkout), persisted at `.runtime/app_settings.json` and overlaid on CiaoConfig. `CiaoConfig.from_env` reads the bind address and log level from the same file with the side-effect-free `read_app_settings`, and server startup imports any `PWA_HOST`/`CIAO_LOG_LEVEL`/`CIAO_DEV_MODE`/`CIAO_APP_REPO` still in `.env` once (`legacy_env_imported`). A stored global `insights_model` (retired) is moved on load into the slot of the provider it named (`opencode:<id>` to opencode, a bare id to Claude) unless that provider already has its own, then the file is rewritten without it. A stored `font_scale` of 1.2, the default before 1.15, is moved to 1.15 once (`font_scale_migrated`). Edited via Settings → General and Settings → Providers.
  webhooks.py                  Private webhook trigger configuration and its separately revocable secrets (#981, child A1 of #974). A foundation only: a frozen `WebhookTrigger` record, a schema-1 store at an explicit `path` (the engine's file is `<runtime>/webhooks.json`, built per request by `routes_webhooks.py` and by the `routes_hooks.py` receiver), and SHA-256 credential verifiers. Only the verifier is stored; a raw secret is returned exactly once, from `create`/`rotate_secret`, and never reaches the file, a public record, a `repr` or an error. New triggers are disabled and carry no permission mode of their own (an event's chat takes the new-chat default, `bypass` included when that is the default), caller-supplied prompts are not representable, input-policy is event-text only, revocation destroys the verifier (so re-enabling restores nothing) while rotation only replaces it, every mutation re-reads the file under `keyed_lock` plus an owner-private advisory sibling lock and writes through `mkstemp_private` + fsync + `os.replace`, and a corrupt or newer-schema file is raised rather than reset. The **receiver** (`#1010`, child A3) lives here too, beside the store: `WebhookReceiver` plus the append-only journal `<runtime>/webhook-receipts.jsonl`, modelled on `memory_receipts.py` (private lock, `fsync` per row, latest row per id wins) rather than on the fail-open `job_runs.py`. One durable `WebhookReceipt` per accepted event, with a stable id derived from `(trigger_id, idempotency_key)` so a retry collapses instead of duplicating, a SHA-256 request digest so the same key with a *different* body is an `idempotency_conflict` rather than a second event, and the sender's event text as the only body field — `{"text": ...}` and nothing else, which is what makes "a sender cannot choose the workspace, project, model or permission" a property of the shape rather than a blocklist of field names to keep current. Bounds are `MAX_BODY_BYTES` 65536, `RATE_LIMIT_PER_MINUTE` 10 on an in-process per-trigger sliding window (the precedent being `routes_auth.py`'s login window; `ciao/rate_limits.py` is quota telemetry, not a limiter), `MAX_PENDING_RECEIPTS` 20 open receipts per trigger, and a 7-day dedupe retention after which the key is generation-stepped rather than folded onto a settled receipt. Statuses are `accepted → launching → launched | failed | interrupted`: the `accepted` row is durable before any launch is attempted, the `launching` allocation before the model turn, and the `launched` outcome (carrying the chat id in `detail`) after it, so a crash in the ambiguous window is recorded `interrupted` (needs review) by `recover_interrupted`, never silently replayed, while a success stays a success instead of occupying the trigger's `MAX_PENDING_RECEIPTS` slots. `accepted_receipts(limit=…)` is the dispatcher's startup-sweep read. **The history read (#1044)** is the other half of the journal's life: `read_recent_rows` walks the file backwards in `_REVERSE_WINDOW_BYTES` windows instead of folding all of it as a dedupe decision must, keeps one row per receipt id — walking backwards the first row seen for an id *is* its effective state, which is what makes stopping at `RECEIPTS_HISTORY_LIMIT` (50) honest — and `WebhookReceipt.to_public_dict` is the only shape that leaves the engine. The store itself launches nothing: `begin_launch`/`settle_launched`/`settle_failed` belong to `webhook_dispatch.py`. Two wirings sit outside it: `routes_api.py` calls `revoke_workspace` while unregistering a workspace (revoked before the registry entry goes, and rolling the archive back if it cannot, so a restored workspace name reactivates no old credential), and `main.py` runs `webhook_dispatch.resume_pending` once at boot so receipts left `accepted` or `launching` by a crash are dispatched or recorded `interrupted`. See `docs/WEBHOOK_TRIGGER_STORE.md`.
  webhook_dispatch.py          Webhook dispatch (#1020, child A4 of #974): one accepted `WebhookReceipt` in, one ordinary project chat out, settled durably as `launched`/`failed`/`interrupted`. `dispatch_receipt` resolves the trigger's pinned target — an explicit `project_id` must exist *and* belong to the trigger's workspace, `null` means that workspace's General, and a deleted/foreign/no-General target settles `failed` rather than falling back, and a trigger that was deleted or disabled/revoked between accepting and dispatching settles `failed` too, so the off switch reaches an event already in the journal — creates the chat titled for the trigger with **no** `mode`/`model`/`provider`, so it takes the operator's new-chat defaults, and calls `pcm.start_stream(chat_id, prompt)` with **no `unattended` argument**, which is the whole no-escalation property (the flag is what makes a turn unattended and its approval requests denied) so approval cards are ordinary Needs-you cards. The prompt is the trigger's `instructions` followed by the sender's `event_text` quoted inside a fixed `<webhook-event>` fence with the tag escaped inside the text, so a payload cannot read as an instruction. `begin_launch` is durable before `start_stream` and `settle_launched` records the chat once it returns, and `resume_pending` (called once from `main.py` at startup, and only outside bootstrap mode) runs `recover_interrupted` first and then dispatches what a restart left `accepted`, bounded by `MAX_RESUMED_LAUNCHES`. Dispatch is idempotent by receipt state *and* claims the receipt in an in-process `_IN_FLIGHT` set before its first `await`, because the state check is a read-then-write across awaits and the startup sweep's accepted list can be read while a route-scheduled task is dispatching the same receipt — a sweep runs each receipt once and a stranded `launching` is never replayed. The `WebhookDispatchHost` protocol declares `start_stream` without `unattended` so the rule is type-checked rather than commented. Store and journal calls go through `asyncio.to_thread` (file lock + fsync); the chat manager is called on the event loop, which owns the broker and the event hub.
  error_log.py                 Rotating file handler for server ERROR+ logs (`server_errors.log`), plus an opt-in DEBUG sibling (`server_debug.log`, enabled by the `debug` log level in Settings) surfaced through the debug issue report. Consumed by the error-triage schedule and the debug issue report.
  debug_report.py              Aggregate runtime issues (server error log + failed job runs) for the dev-mode `GET /api/debug/issues` endpoint and the engine-wide `{{ISSUE_REPORT}}` dispatch of trusted system schedules, and build the workspace-scoped failed-job report behind `ciao context logs`: recorded errors are redacted with `scoped_failure_error`, and the prompt-facing body is truncated to 64,000 characters with an explicit marker before fencing.
  startup_triage.py            Startup error triage: harvest runtime errors into a fix-it chat through the schedule pipeline.
  setup_marker.py              First-time-setup receipt (`.runtime/setup-completed-at`, written only when setup creates a workspace's `.env` from scratch). Startup reads it to hold system-routine catch-up for 24h. The onboarding journey is the single automatic first-run entry; interview and knowledge-seeding prompts remain available on request. Also `.runtime/welcome-pending`, written only when setup generated the PWA password and deleted on dismiss or password change; Home's welcome card is gated on it.
  job_runs.py                  Fail-open recorder for background-job runs. Appends one JSON record per run to `.runtime/job_runs.jsonl` (size-trimmed like `error_log.py`) plus a latest-run index, read by the debug report and the workspace audit. Background tasks wrap their work in `track()`/`track_sync()`; `RETIRED_JOBS` hides runs of jobs the code no longer has.
  models.py                    Shared data models (ChatContext, AgentRequest, etc.).
  model_tiers.py               Claude's tier vocabulary (haiku / sonnet / opus / fable) recognized as bare model aliases; no cross-provider mapping tables.
  migration_notices.py         The migration notices, each probed once (#816, extended by #833 and #800's last slice). Three surfaces answer "what did an upgrade leave here?": the Home strip (`operator_actions.detect_actions`, polled every 60s and on focus), the `upgrade_notices` section of `os_audit` (a diagnostic, never red), and since #833 the "After this update" catalog, whose card is an offer the operator may dismiss where the report is not theirs to silence. They used to hold separate copies of two conditions, and the copies drifted: the vault-location predicate existed twice, and the audit's remedy taught a hand migration (move the folder, hand-edit the registry) that the engine refuses and that `docs/VAULT_MIGRATION_PROMPT.md` no longer teaches; the wikilink notice had two *applicability* rules, Home asking for an adopted vault and the audit asking only for a receipt, so the two surfaces answered different questions about the same vault. Here each probe owns its condition, its applicability and every sentence about it, and every surface references them. `vault_location_findings` is two registry-resolved paths and one `is_dir()` — no scan — and its remedy is the managed `ciao vault-relocate` sentence with its undo, its restart, and the shapes `--apply` refuses (symlink, non-empty destination, a vault root that IS the install root, a shared root claimed twice, a nested destination, uncommitted changes, a vault outside the install's git worktree), stating a manual route only for the two refusals that really carry one and leaving the rest to the preview, which is `vault_relocate.plan`'s authority and not this notice's to pre-empt. The wikilink notice applies when no **completed** link-migration receipt exists (`read_receipt` verifies the canonical vault identity and completed migration status, so a half-finished or foreign run does not silence it) **and** a walk actually found a wikilink; there is no `vault_mode` gate, because a scratch vault is the one an operator can hand a wikilink and the audit losing that is not a parity win. Who may establish the second half is the whole of the cost difference, and it is named functions rather than a flag: `resolve_links` walks (the audit, which is already a full-install pass, and the off-loop refresh) and publishes what it found; `cached_links` only reads that (Home — the strip may not walk, on open, on focus or on the poll); `start_links_scan` puts the walk on `async_reads.run_read` — coalesced, admission-capped, never on the event loop — starts it DETACHED from `GET /api/housekeeping` exactly as `_cached_update_hint` starts the release lookup, keeps one in flight at a time, observes the task's outcome rather than leaving it for the garbage collector, and is torn down by `shutdown_links_scan`, which `ciao/main.py` registers ahead of the read-executor close. A cold engine therefore shows no card until the next poll picks the verdict up. The third notice, `unrehomed_people`, has no walk at all: `rehomed_people_finding` is a registry read plus a completed-receipt read through `completed_rehome` (which is `vault_rehome.read_receipt`, so a `partial` run keeps the finding and a receipt predating the `status` field clears it), gated on more than one registered workspace because a single workspace has no counterpart for a person note to be misfiled FROM — an unactionable offer is how a fresh install learns to ignore the strip. That answer stays **broad and advisory on purpose**, because it is a diagnostic: it cannot know anything is misfiled, which is why the wording says *may* be. Since #833 it also backs an install-scoped update task, the first notice whose Home surface is a *dismissible card* rather than a tile — and the card deliberately asks a **different, harder** question. `rehome_legacy_candidates` runs `vault_rehome.plan_rehome` on the shared vault (the same plan the operator's preview prints) and offers only a `mechanical` candidate, because a receipt check is satisfied for free by every fresh install that never needed the migration: #833's review caught exactly that false positive. Four gates run before the walk and each one *proves* a mechanical candidate is unreachable — a completed receipt (the remedy refuses a second pass, so the card would be unactionable), one workspace, no shared vault directory (per-root has no workspace segment, so `vault-rehome` cannot act there at all and the task must not exist), and no bound tag role at all (`detect_misfiled_people` needs a tag naming a role that is bound; ONE bound role is enough, and a gate asking for two was a false negative on `[work, clientA]` — found by R2 review — because a note filed under a workspace that plays no role has no `own_role` to disagree with). The asymmetry is pinned both ways — task offered ⇒ audit reports, audit silent ⇒ task not offered — and nothing in this module reads an update-task record, so dismissing the card silences the card and never the report. `tests/test_unrehomed_people_update_task.py` pins the fresh-install silence, the mechanical candidate, the per-root silence without a walk, every receipt shape against completion, a hand-written `completed` record with no receipt on disk, and that the one walk runs off the loop through `run_read` once per window. A published answer is one of three states — `LINKS_FOUND`, `LINKS_CLEAN`, `LINKS_FAILED` — and only `found` is ever a finding: a walk that could not finish is an unknown, cached as such so a broken vault is not re-walked and re-logged every 60s, and never as `clean`, which would be the notice quietly lying. `LINKS_SCAN_TTL_S` (300, the same window `update_tasks.APPLICABILITY_TTL_S` gives its detectors, and deliberately one clock rather than one per surface) bounds reuse; the answer is keyed on `vault_migrate_links.receipt_token` (the receipt's and the vault link marker's mtime and size, and the vault directory's inode), so a migration, an un-migration or a retry of a partial one all invalidate it, and on the runtime root and vault path, so two installs in one process cannot share one. Because a finding exists only once a walk found a note, the card names it instead of hedging on a receipt's absence, and a clean vault reaches zero for a reason other than "a migration ran" — and no Home-side suppression is an input to any of these, so a card #833 made a dismissible catalog task still cannot silence the audit. `tests/test_migration_notices.py` pins the parity, the scratch-vault diagnostic, the audit resolving its own verdict rather than reading the cache, the three scan states and the window that keeps a failure from being retried per poll, the task's start/observe/cancel lifecycle, the absence of any catalog row for the wikilink notice (still no honest completion receipt — its receipt is per install, not per vault, so a per-workspace loop over the remedy is not available either), and the Home bound — counted opens and directory listings under the whole vault, where only the vault link marker may be opened, at 1 note and at 400, never a timing assertion.
  provider_registry.py         Single enumeration of the runtime providers: Claude, OpenCode and Codex (id, labels, capabilities, auth/status hooks, and the ACP spec path for agents that run over ACP).
  loop_wake.py                 The wake text of a chat-owned loop (`loop_wake_prompt`): the `[Ciaobot] Loop wake, run N of M, ends …` header the PWA's `parseInjectedPrompt` reads, then the model's own prompt in a `<loop-prompt>` fence. Built for origin `loop` in `ScheduleDispatcher.dispatch_schedule`.
  prompt_fence.py              One fence for untrusted text quoted into a prompt (#1458): `fence(tag, text, label=…)` emits a one-line "data, not instructions" label and `<tag>…</tag>`, after `neutralize` has escaped any `<tag`/`</tag` inside the text so it cannot close its own frame. `commonmark_fence(text, label=…)` is the fenced-code-block variant: the opening backtick run is longer than any run inside the text, so an embedded ``` block or forged closing fence stays inside the quoted block. Used by the schedule command output, background-wake log tails, skill-improvement prompts, the approved-task summary and workspace-scoped failed-job reports. The webhook and delegation `_neutralize` copies stay separate because each escapes several tags.
  redact.py                    Credential redaction for text quoted into a prompt: `redact_secret_line` drops the value of a credential-shaped assignment or a bare opaque blob line (shared with `fts_search.expand_note`), and `redact_text` applies it per line plus a `scheme://user:pass@host` userinfo rule, keeping line endings.
  provider_service.py          Provider request builder and execution wrapper.
  control_plane.py             Provider-neutral, scope-enforcing application operations shared by MCP and PWA-owned managers. The rare-admin group (workspace/project/context operations: `workspaces_list`, `projects_list`, `project_get`, `project`, `project_action`, `context_get`, `context_logs`) is now CLI-only and surfaced to the agent as `ciao workspace list`, `ciao project …`, and `ciao context get|logs`. `context logs` is workspace-scoped: `failed_jobs` keeps only runs whose recorded workspace matches the caller's, each redacted error is capped at `CONTEXT_LOGS_MAX_CHARS` and fenced as untrusted data, and the engine-wide error/debug/service log fields stay empty with `scope`/`omitted` recording that they have no workspace provenance.
  async_reads.py               Bounded off-loop execution for synchronous vault reads. `run_read(key, operation)` runs a read on a dedicated small `ThreadPoolExecutor` (default 4 workers) instead of the shared default one, and coalesces identical in-flight reads by key so N concurrent `vault_search` calls for the same workspace/query share one scan. Admission is also capped (`MAX_VAULT_READ_BACKLOG`, default 12): `ThreadPoolExecutor`'s queue is unbounded and a cancelled caller deliberately leaves its read running, so a burst of distinct keys or disconnected callers would otherwise pile up full-vault scans; over the cap a caller waits for a slot without blocking the loop, and same-key callers never consume a slot. The server registers `shutdown_vault_read_executor()` as a shutdown callback, so a restart discards the queued backlog (`cancel_futures=True`) instead of joining it. The worker owns its SQLite connection end to end (opened and closed there), so no connection crosses threads. `asyncio.to_thread` cannot be cancelled, so a cancelled awaiter only detaches: the worker runs to completion, stays joinable by a later caller under the same key, and its exception is always observed (`_observe_failure`) so a detached failure is never silent. Each caller awaits its own bridge future fed by a done-callback, never the shared future, so cancelling one waiter cannot cancel the work other same-key waiters depend on. `keyed_lock(key)` is a process-wide lock used to serialize the write phase of concurrent index passes against one SQLite database file (SQLite takes one file-level write lock), while the read-only search phase stays concurrent; the archive postprocess takes the same lock when it indexes a newly archived file. `reset_vault_read_executor()` is the test isolation hook.
  workspaces.py                Shared logical-workspace registry rules (validation, serialization, persistence) used by the PWA routes and the control-plane workspace tools. Private registry serialization retains the engine-generated lifetime ID for each linked shared brain; public Settings projections omit it.
  mcp_server.py                Agent control-plane core: the shared operation table (`OPERATIONS`, one entry per operation with name, `_READ`/`_WRITE`/`_DESTRUCTIVE` annotations, docstring, and the `(service, **kwargs)` function), the `AgentSessionRegistry` bearer-token verifier, and telemetry. Ciaobot keeps no external provider MCP inventory or status: each CLI owns its MCP configuration. The MCP adapter was removed in S6; the agent dispatcher (`ciao/agent_surface.py`) resolves the same table, so argument validation is shared by construction. There is no argv auto-approval: every `ciao …` shell command keeps a card in `auto` mode (an argv allow prefix is a shell-suffix bypass risk), and users switch to `bypass` for no cards.
  signals.py                   Restart / deploy signals.
  instance_lock.py             Process-lifetime lock for one backend per runtime directory (`.runtime/server.lock`).
  agent_paths.py               Where the provider CLIs keep per-workspace state (D-03/D-04, #696), checked against recorded observations in `tests/fixtures/agent_discovery/`. `claude_project_slug(path)`: Claude Code's `~/.claude/projects/<slug>` name — every character that is not an ASCII letter or digit becomes `-`, on every OS (`/Users/me/ciao` → `-Users-me-ciao`, `C:\dev\ciaobot` → `C--dev-ciaobot`). `claude_global_config_path()` (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_PATH`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `~/.claude.json`).
  supervise.py                 `ciao supervise`: the cross-platform supervisor. Runs `ciao run --supervised` as a child (spawned with `tree_spawn_options(dies_with_engine=True)`, so a service manager's group kill still reaches it) and owns the relaunch loop — exit code 75 relaunches, any other code is returned, SIGTERM/SIGINT forward `ProcessTree.terminate()` and then `kill()` after `STOP_GRACE_S`. A crash loop of more than `CRASH_LOOP_MAX_RESTARTS` *consecutive* restarts waits `BACKOFF_INITIAL_S`, doubling, capped at `BACKOFF_MAX_S`; a child that ran longer than `CRASH_LOOP_WINDOW_S` did real work first, so its restart starts the count over. The port-in-use code (69, see `port_in_use.py`) is the one exception to "any other code is returned": the supervisor *holds* the job instead of exiting, because the service manager owns this process and one that respawns on every exit (launchd's `KeepAlive`) would put the engine straight back into the same bind failure, forever (#1654). CLI: `ciao supervise [-- <ciao run args>]`. On macOS it first runs the `workspace_access` probe.
  workspace_access.py          darwin-only probe `ciao supervise` runs before anything touches the workspace; an EPERM (macOS Files & Folders denial) writes one line to `~/.local/state/ciaobot/workspace-access-denied.txt`, prints it and exits 78 so launchd retries on its throttle; a passing probe deletes the file; `ciao service status` and the installer show it.
  os_support/                  The one home for platform differences that are not provider-specific (#696); call sites import from here and never branch on `sys.platform`. `locks.py`: `lock_exclusive(fd, *, blocking)` / `unlock(fd)` with `flock` semantics — `fcntl.flock` (contention raises `BlockingIOError`). `users.py`: `user_key()` tells local accounts apart in shared per-user paths — the uid. `files.py`: `open_fd(path, flags, mode, *, follow_symlinks=True)` is `os.open` with `O_NOFOLLOW` when links are not followed. `links.py`: `link_dir`/`link_file`/`is_link`/`points_to`/`link_source`/`link_target`/`remove_link` for the `sync_skills` catalog mirrors — relative symlinks. `child_env.py`: `child_env(source=None)` is the environment an engine child is started with: ACP agents and their one-shots, the OpenCode server, native and PTY terminals, scheduled commands, workflow bash nodes, the provider and setup probes, the `auth login`/`logout` commands, the git helpers (local and network alike), the PWA snapshot and pull, LibreOffice previews, the OpenCode import probes and the adapter npm installs. It is a copy of the source (default `os.environ`). When `package_version.detect_install_mode()` is `bundled_app` it **unsets** `SSL_CERT_FILE` and `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE` for the child: a child does not get the certifi path, and a user's own `SSL_CERT_FILE` is not restored, because the Swift side strips it before the engine starts. Nothing rebuilds the user's value. The engine's own `os.environ` is never changed (its httpx TLS stays on certifi), and every other install mode passes the environment through unchanged. npm needs no exception: Node ignores `SSL_CERT_FILE` unless built with `--use-openssl-ca`, and `npm view`/`npm install --package-lock-only` gave identical results with the variable unset, set to certifi, or set to a missing file. `processes.py`: spawn with `**tree_spawn_options()` and wrap the pid in `ProcessTree`, whose `terminate()`/`kill()` reach every descendant — `start_new_session` + `killpg(SIGTERM/SIGKILL)`. A `dies_with_engine=True` child (the OpenCode server) stays in the engine's own process group, as before, so launchd and a terminal's Ctrl-C still reach it, and `terminate`/`kill` signal that one pid. `terminal.py`: the one terminal seam (#1724) — the `TerminalProcess` protocol (`read`/`write`/`resize`/`poll`/`close`, plus the `pid`) and `spawn_terminal(cwd=..., cols=80, rows=24, argv=None)`, which starts the POSIX adapter (a real pty) and imports it only when a terminal is asked for; it adds no validation and no retry, so a caller never asks which platform it is on. `folder_io.py` (#3604): descriptor-relative no-clobber writes for an ordinary linked folder — `open_chain` opens the root and each parent with `O_NOFOLLOW|O_DIRECTORY` and returns their `(st_dev, st_ino)` chain, `revalidate_chain` re-opens the visible path before and after a mutation so a renamed-away parent is detected, `open_or_create_private_dir` creates a missing staging directory or opens an existing one with `O_NOFOLLOW` and strict owner/mode/ACL validation without chmod'ing it, and `create_staging_dir`/`create_staged_file` stage the note in that trusted storage (engine state when it is on the destination volume, otherwise a `0o700` `.ciao-share-staging` directory at the destination mount root outside every linked tree) and tag it with a random operation identity in an extended attribute, `verified_hardlink_support`/`verified_rename_observability` probe real primitives before effect, `same_mount` and `link_no_clobber` publish that exact inode with one `link(2)` only when staging and destination share a filesystem (so an existing destination is never replaced), and `discard_staging` removes a source only when its inode still matches and otherwise leaves it (and any replacement) alone; `rmdir` cannot remove a file. Nothing in the shared tree is ever created, unlinked, renamed or rmdir'd for staging or cleanup. A cross-device link or a volume that cannot carry the operation tag raises `UnsupportedFilesystem`, and the caller asks for a manual export rather than claiming success. There is no replace or rename primitive because an ordinary folder has no portable compare-and-swap. `private.py`: `make_private`/`make_private_dir`/`is_private`/`open_private`/`carry_mode` — `0o600`/`0o700` mode bits. The legacy answers remain mode-only; `is_private_descriptor_strict` additionally require the inode's effective owner and, on macOS, refuse an extended ACL allow entry for another principal, while `is_owned_descriptor`/`descriptor_acl_grants_another_account` expose the pieces for ancestor-directory checks. Linux POSIX ACLs are not inspected. `tool_path.py`: `terminal_path()` is the PATH the user's own terminal resolves against (an interactive login shell), plus `resolve_executable(cmd, path=...)`, which resolves a command against it, and `resolve_command(cmd, path=...)`, the argv prefix that runs it. `limits.py`: `raise_file_descriptor_limit(target=SERVER_NOFILE_TARGET)` raises the process's soft `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, clamped to the hard limit, and is called early in `main.py::_run_server_locked` before uvicorn binds. A service manager starts the engine with a low soft limit (macOS launchd's default is 256) unless the packaged unit raises it, so the in-process raise makes `ciao run`, `ciao supervise` and every service manager start with the same budget, independent of whether an upgrade rewrote the unit. Best-effort: a `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` failure is logged and swallowed. `terminal_posix.py` / `terminal_child.py`: the POSIX terminal adapter (#1692) — a real pty with a real controlling terminal, still internal (no route, no public API). The engine is threaded, so it does not fork: it opens a pty, spawns a fresh `-I -S` helper with `start_new_session=True`, and the helper claims the slave with `TIOCSCTTY` and `exec`s the shell, so the shell keeps the pid, the session and the process group `Popen` created (no second `setsid`, no shell child of its own). The environment is the canonical one (`host_terminal._terminal_env`, the engine's minus secrets) plus `TERM`, with `COLUMNS`/`LINES` dropped; there is no second secret filter. `read` is non-blocking (`None` = nothing yet, `b""` = EOF), `write` reports what it took and buffers nothing, `resize` is `tcsetwinsize`. Ctrl-C is the byte `0x03` to the tty, so the line discipline interrupts the foreground job, not the shell's group. Because an interactive shell puts every job in its own group, `close` lists the processes (`/bin/ps`, absolute path, bounded), authorizes each candidate by `os.getsid(candidate) == pid` and re-checks the representative immediately before `killpg`, so a process that left the session is very unlikely to be signalled; the check and the signal are not atomic, so a pid the system reused can still slip through in between. The engine's own group is never in that session and is never touched. Deliberately daemonized processes (their own session) are outside that guarantee: this is not a sandbox. The I/O calls and the close share one lock, so the pty descriptor cannot be closed while a call is using it and its number cannot be handed to something else in between; the close is serialized, so a second caller waits for the first. A cleanup that cannot finish is reported as a failure and reported again on every later close, never as a success: an EPERM is accepted only when a bounded status listing proves the group holds nothing but zombies, and a shell that stays unreaped is said so. A spawn that fails after the child existed is undone by that same session-wide cleanup, not by a weaker one, and the helper reports a failed claim or a failed exec with a status instead of a traceback in the user's terminal.
  execution_modes.py           Claude/opencode provider approval policies and the CLI control-plane posture. Since S6 there is no argv allow/ask prefix list (`AGENT_CLI_ALLOW_PATTERNS` / `AGENT_CLI_ASK_PATTERNS` were removed with the argv auto-approval): every `ciao …` shell command keeps a card in `auto` mode (an argv allow prefix is a shell-suffix bypass risk), and users switch to `bypass` for no cards. The control plane keeps its own mode gates and `unattended_forbidden`.
  tool_path.py                 Resolve external CLI tools against the user's real terminal PATH (Homebrew, nvm, `~/.local/bin`, opencode's `~/.opencode/bin`). It merges that PATH with the process one and resolves against the result; what "the user's terminal" means — and what a tool resolves to — is `os_support/tool_path.py`, so nothing here branches on the platform. The POSIX terminal-PATH probe spawns an interactive login shell (~0.74s), so it is cached against a fingerprint of the files such a shell reads — the rc candidates plus `$ZDOTDIR`'s, `/etc/paths`, `/etc/paths.d/*` and `$SHELL` — rather than a TTL: the setup wizard polls every 2s and a saved rc file must still be picked up on the next poll. Concurrent probes share one spawn.
  git_proc.py                  Timeout-safe git subprocess spawning for `local_session`: git runs in its own process tree (`os_support.processes`) so a timeout kills the forked `ssh` too, and the child is reaped and its pipes closed (a bare `proc.kill()` leaked 2 fds per timeout — issue #470). `run_git_sync` is the read-only counterpart, for questions answerable off-loop.
  git_mutation.py              The one serialization point for git mutations (#674). `repository_mutation(workspace)` is an async context manager holding a task-reentrant lock keyed by the canonical work-tree top level, so manual sync, the post-conflict resync, the background backup push and admin snapshots cannot interleave git commands in one checkout, while two unrelated repositories run concurrently. Nested helpers in the SAME task reenter (a child task still waits); the lock is released in a `finally` and its registry entry dropped as soon as nothing holds or waits on it. `ensure_mutable` is the external-state half: it reports a preexisting `index.lock` or an in-progress merge/rebase — resolved through `git rev-parse --git-path`, so a linked worktree finds its own — and never removes it. Callers turn that refusal into their existing return shape, including an error response from the admin snapshot route.
  repo_location.py             Derives where a chat in a linked project worked (toplevel, branch, worktree, exists) from the turn's file touches and shell dirs via bounded async git.
  shared_brain.py              Shared brains: Git checkouts or ordinary folders a workspace links for reference (`kind=git|folder`, omitted kind means legacy Git). Folder roots are canonical accessible directories outside the install/runtime/vault roots; Git identity and update checks are never run for them. `update_brain` and `update_loop` fast-forward only clean Git checkouts, never rewriting the folder. The server starts the periodic loop at the serve boundary, and `shutdown_updates` closes scheduled-update admission, cancels and awaits the periodic and per-link tasks before the event loop closes. Each persisted link also has an engine-generated lifetime ID so unlink/relink cannot inherit consent; changing kind requires unlink/relink.
  shared_brain_authority.py    Steward authority core for one shared brain (#3615), before routes or Settings: privileged entry points take only sealed `AuthenticatedPrincipal` capabilities minted by the injected `AuthorityBackend`, never caller-supplied names; bootstrap nominates a first steward who must accept with their own capability, transfer requires the authenticated incumbent at the observed generation, and recovery requires an authenticated administrator, explicit confirmation and successor acceptance. Every invitation is bound to brain, storage, actor, successor, operation, generation, expiry and a single-use nonce, and each transition rotates the generation and invalidates unspent invitations. Every mutation also needs a short-lived single-use `MutationAdmission` from `AuthorityBackend.admit`, applied through `AuthorityBackend.commit` under the provider's enforceable fence, because a permission lookup before a local compare-and-swap cannot fence external revocation. With no backend, privileged transitions fail closed with `provider_fencing_unavailable`; an unverifiable folder or generic connector stays member-only; `ProtectedAuthorityStore` is only the conditional owner-private record store (`0600`/`0700`, strict descriptor owner/mode/platform-ACL checks on the directory and document, `create_if_absent`, `replace_if_generation`, and a directory `fsync` after the atomic rename), not a provider fence. Production activation gates (credential authentication, shared atomic admissions, provider fencing, durable audit/crash recovery) are documented in the module docstring. Tests: `tests/test_shared_brain_authority.py`.
  shared_brain_guide.py        Reads one brain's bounded, versioned `CIAO-CONTENT-GUIDE.md`, pins brain identity/version/exact-byte digest, and resolves a typed section or collection destination only when an independent per-brain consent mapping allows the same type, kind, path, section and operation. Any malformed type entry fails the whole guide closed; UTF-8-unencodable paths, headings or text (escaped lone surrogates) are malformed, and malformed, unsupported, missing or changed guides are unusable until reviewed, so a guide edit cannot widen an existing grant. `resolve_for_publication` makes the stored pin mandatory; persistent consent/link lifetime, final-write revalidation and Settings remain #3615 integration gates.
  share_proposals.py           Qualifies one exact Markdown note against a linked shared brain's tracked upstream or a new note in an ordinary folder (a folder update is refused until #3626), keeps immutable review artifacts in private runtime state, and files only an opaque `[share]` queue row. It never publishes; the human consent flow owns that later step. Undo drafts use the same store and stable receipt-derived request keys.
  share_publish.py             Publishes an attended, queue-bound share artifact using an exact review token and link lifetime, a frozen destination, bounded non-force pushes or a descriptor-relative no-clobber folder create with an operation-tagged staging inode, persisted attempts and idempotent receipts. Shares checkout serialization with `update_brain`; receipt metadata is projected into history and memory receipt APIs while review text stays in its private runtime artifact.
  repo_link.py                 Links a project to a local git checkout: `validate_repo_path` (exists, is a directory, equals `git rev-parse --show-toplevel`; stores git's resolved toplevel), `repo_state` (`{path, branch, dirty, missing}`; `status` runs with `--no-optional-locks`, every git call has a 5 s timeout), `repo_payload` (`repo_state` plus `instructions: {name, chars} | null` from `read_repo_instructions`, which `ProjectInfo.to_dict()` reports as `repo`) and `candidates_payload` (git checkouts one level under the hardcoded roots ~/repos, ~/code, ~/src, ~/dev, ~/Developer, ~/projects). `ProjectInfo.repo_path` persists the link; `to_dict()` reports `repo`.
  local_session.py             Current-branch git sync flow (LocalSessionManager): preflight, commit + pull + push, conflict chat.
