A record also carries its `SkillOrigin` list — one entry per finding, naming the `learning_id` it came from, the `source_revision` of that learning's own canonical line at filing (since #728-E: `learning_records.entry_revision`, the hash of *its* line rather than of the whole `Learnings.md`, so an unrelated edit elsewhere in the document no longer cancels eligibility for every learning at once), and the state of *that* finding — because a record is one row per skill and so aggregates findings: settlement is per origin, and a linked origin can only be `applied` with a verification on record or `dismissed` by an explicit rejection. `learning_settlement` folds every origin naming a learning (and its `aliases`) across every record in the workspace, settled ones included, and `learning_cleanup_eligibility` is the hook `ciao/learnings_cleanup.py` asks per learning; it returns a reason and removes nothing. An origin whose recorded revision does not match the entry's own line revision is reported as changed-since and kept, never rewritten to match the present file.
  dependency_updates.py        Deterministic PyPI/npm manifest and registry helpers used by release preparation. The weekly dependency-changelog review is workspace-owned and is not shipped as public app code.
  upstream_drafts.py           The `[review]` drafts for lessons whose target this workspace does not own (#728-D), i.e. the two routing destinations that are not an edit to an owned source. A `new_skill` draft carries a `recurrence` block (#3238-B): `file_draft` refuses it unless the procedure is a learning at the revision the caller read and three distinct tasks back it, and the supported completion ids are stored as `completion_id` on the existing `origins` entries, so no sidecar schema changed; a memory pass over an approved task has no manual route to a new skill without that block (`new_skill_pass_refusal`). The pass-chat check reads `CIAO_CHAT_ID`, which is plain environment, so it is advisory: it stops a pass from filing without a block, not a process that sets the variable itself. An unreadable chat store fails closed (`ChatStoreUnreadable`). `UpstreamDraft` is one record per `(target, skill, change)` in a workspace, stored as JSON under `<vault>/Workspace/Skill-Drafts/`, and the review queue gets a one-line `[review <id>]` bullet beside it — a bullet cannot hold a target, a sanitized public body and a private provenance, so the line names the record and the record holds the rest, exactly as a `[note_edit]` row names its sidecar. `file_draft` deduplicates on the *change*, not the sighting, so a nightly run that reaches the same conclusion from a different transcript folds into the record already queued; evidence and learning links accumulate and the lifecycle never moves, because filing is a question. `sanitize_lesson` is the gate on the public half and it refuses rather than redacts — a transcript excerpt, a chat or vault path, an absolute home path, a credential or an email fails the whole body by name — while `private_evidence` keeps the local provenance either way, so "may not publish" is never "lose the finding". `approve_draft`, `create_new_skill` and `reject_draft` are attended only, and the enforcement is `unattended_run`: they read the vault's own curation lease, so an unattended Workspace care run is refused with `UnattendedRefused` (`ciao skill-draft-approve` / `skill-draft-reject` exit 4) rather than having to pass a flag it could also forget to pass — the deferral `memory_policy.UNATTENDED_DEFERRED_ACTIONS` already states. `approve_draft` **searches before it creates**, and both calls name the same `--repo`: a dedupe that looked in a different repository than the filing is not a dedupe. One matching open issue is linked, more than one is an ambiguity that holds the draft pending rather than a guess, a `gh` failure returns the draft unchanged so the retry searches again instead of duplicating, and a draft naming no owning repository files nothing rather than letting `gh` default to the current directory. A rejected change is never re-filed. `create_new_skill` is the other half: `skills_inventory.create_owned_skill` writes `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` under the same confinement the resolver enforces, refusing any name that exists as a directory, a symlink or an installed copy, then the readback is checked and the workspace synced, and only then does the row settle — a sync that did not finish leaves the draft pending with the reason on it, and a re-approve resumes by skipping the creation when `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` already holds exactly the submitted content — so the new-skill path never aims the edit-only `skill-proposal-add` at a target that does not exist. Shell: `ciao skill-draft-add`, `ciao skill-drafts`, `ciao skill-draft-approve`, `ciao skill-draft-reject`.
   skills_inventory.py          Build the Settings skill inventory from workspace skills (custom) and packaged stock skills, with installed Claude badges. Also the one home of `MAX_SKILL_BYTES`, the 15KB context budget for a single `SKILL.md`, and the one write: `create_owned_skill`, which writes a new `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` for the new-skill routing path (#728-D) and returns it read back through `resolve_owned_skill`. It is a separate function rather than a mode on the resolver because that resolver's contract is that an *existing* owned source is the only thing a proposal may be filed against, and a flag that returned a path for a directory that is not there would take that contract away from every other caller. It refuses a name that already exists as a directory, a symlink or an installed copy (a creation that overwrote one would be an edit wearing a creation's permissions) and a name the frontmatter does not match, since a skill is loaded under its frontmatter name; `MAX_SKILL_BYTES` is reported as `over_budget` rather than refused, because the budget warns everywhere else.
     sync_skills.py               Install packaged stock skills and mirror workspace assets into provider-native catalogs. Stock commands seeded into `commands/` carry a sibling `.ciao-stock-command` marker holding the sha256 of the last written stock text, so unmodified copies are refreshed on every sync while user-edited ones are unmarked and never touched again. CLI: `ciao sync-skills`.
   skill_import.py              Validated skill import from zip archives (zip-slip, single SKILL.md, frontmatter). Powers POST /api/skills/import. The 15KB SKILL.md budget (`skills_inventory.MAX_SKILL_BYTES`) warns, it does not reject — `os_audit` reports it as over budget, and the memory pass can file a split-it-up proposal.
  cleanup_sdk_blobs.py         Maintenance: drop archived Claude SDK JSONL blobs. CLI: `ciao cleanup-sdk-blobs`.
  label_hygiene.py             Audit open GitHub issue labels against the title-prefix convention documented in `ciao-capabilities/references/support.md` and add missing classification labels. Pure decision function (`plan_label_actions`) separated from the `gh`-shelling side-effecting layer; only ever adds a label, never removes one; idempotent. `[Agent]` and unrecognized/retired prefixes (e.g. `[Report]`) are reported for human decision, not auto-labeled. CLI: `ciao label-hygiene` (dry-run by default, `--apply` writes, `--json` for automation). Not yet wired to a schedule.
  core_prompt.py                `unified_core()` renders the compact Ciaobot core (`_system_instructions()`) for every provider. ACP providers receive it as the stable block of the context capsule (`build_context_capsule(core=...)`), once per provider session; non-ACP drivers still send it as a system prompt until C2b/D2 remove that path. Native provider guide loaders remain the sole source of bounded memory. The core keeps safety and trust boundaries, search/read/abstain recall, write authorization, routing to mandatory skills, and a compact command synopsis. Detailed memory, document and verification procedures live under `ciao-capabilities/references/memory-operations/`; command syntax and output conventions are exposed by `ciao help` from the packaged `ciao-cli` reference (not an installed workspace skill). The chat-first contract (#1599) keeps discussions in chat and canonical-document updates inside authorized document or implementation work; corrections and preferences alone never authorize writes. Deterministic tests establish instruction presence and valid command syntax, not model compliance; live behavioral probes remain separate and bounded. See `docs/AGENT_CLI.md` for prompt layering.
  behavioral_eval.py            Versioned behavioral evaluations for prompts, providers, and guides. Deterministic, model-free contract checks validate the shipped guards (scoped search, control_plane scope, event-shape routing, unattended approval, injection canaries) against the synthetic scenario catalog in `ciao/stock/evals/scenarios.json`; a bounded, credentialed model-backed runner probes one provider/model and records the sha256 of the core prompt, guide fixture, memory region-reconcile prompt, provider, model, and tool catalog with every result. The unattended zero-tolerance check distinguishes a permitted consolidation of existing region entries from promoting a new fact, a malformed structured reply is recorded as one failed scenario rather than aborting the run, and required writes are scored in routing. It never reads a live vault and enforces a declared call/cost ceiling. CLI: `ciao eval contracts|run|compare`.
  task_eval.py                 Agentic task eval: runs real chat turns on an isolated engine (own temp workspace, env stripped of CIAO_/PWA_/GWS_, fixture vault copied in) and grades the resulting workspace deterministically (task list, schedules, projects, files, answer text, task attempts, a declared command). Records tokens, tool calls, ciao-call errors, cost and wall time per run to JSONL; `summarize`/`compare` report medians and deltas. Catalog: `ciao/stock/evals/tasks/`. CLI: `ciao eval tasks list|run|report|compare`. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  task_eval_contract.py        Pure stdlib-only validator/publication seam and pairwise eligibility rules for future privacy-minimized care measurements; it collects nothing, does not authenticate provenance, and is not wired to the legacy eval CLI. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_due.py                  Pure, read-only "is there observed care work" verdict (Empty, Due, Busy, Unavailable) over the worklist `curation-begin` would plan, for `ciao curation-due`. Takes no lease, writes nothing and calls no model; only Empty lets the schedule skip the chat, and every uncertain case fails toward the run that already happens.
  care_eval_fixtures.py        Pure fixed manifest and independent outcome oracle for the nightly, weekly_due and budget_continuation care scenarios; grades declared obligations and protected bytes from harness observations only, creates no files and runs nothing. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_eval_run.py             Schedule-run driver for the care measurement harness: pauses one owned system schedule, triggers the real `POST /api/schedule-run`, and settles on the single job-run row matching its exact dispatch, schedule, workspace and chat. Only `bind_isolated` (a harness-owned loopback engine under a harness workdir) can feed it; boots nothing, runs no model. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_eval_seed.py            Synthetic starting vault builder and effect observer for the care measurement harness: writes one scenario's public seed under a caller-owned layout (the guide at the agent root, the vault beneath it, as a booted engine lays them out), digests it as `fixture_sha256`, and translates the real guide, note-check, proposal-queue and curation-log state into the oracle's symbolic bytes. Also reads the curation cursor and per-item applied receipts for a proven run id. Reads only files under those roots; runs nothing. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_eval_boot.py            Boots one isolated care engine on a caller-supplied starting vault: writes the scenario seed as the task overlay and passes an empty vault fixture, so no generic `_fixture` vault is copied. Starts a process only when called; see `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_eval_record.py          Pure assembly of one care measurement record from a closed schedule-run outcome, the seed manifest and seed observations, validated through `task_eval_contract.public_record`; runs nothing. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_eval_scenario.py        Orchestrates care runs against a caller-owned bound engine: one nightly or weekly_due run verifies the seed on disk, triggers the system schedule, and grades only a settled run; `run_continuation` drives budget_continuation as two attributable attempts (decision D1), grading only attempt 2 and returning `Unavailable` with a fixed reason otherwise. Never boots, stops or calls a model. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
  care_run_outcome.py          Pure view of the last care run's derived outcome: returns planned, completed, deferred, failed, `counts_complete` and capped reasons only when the stored `last_run` was taken by the schedule's own chat (`chat_id` copied from the lease at `curation-begin`); otherwise None. Reads no files; the route supplies the state (#3056 S3).
  care_report.py               Pure fold of a closed care report plan (planned, deferred, blocked keys) and the effective receipts into one outcome per key, with derived counts and a `complete` flag. Reads no files, clock or model; the caller supplies the receipts and each key's operation ids. Writes nothing and is not wired to the schedule yet (#3056 S1).
  care_usage.py                Pure typed projection of attributable care model-call usage: a trusted expected-call inventory plus per-attempt observations keyed by run, worker and call id become per-partition and run-wide coverage, with explicit zero only when observed, dedupe and conflict handling, and no provider adapter, file I/O or savings claim. See `docs/TASK_EVAL.md`.
   memory_tool.py               Bounded memory regions: locate/read/diagnose, atomically prune expired entries, report usage, and apply typed bounded edits in the workspace guide (`AGENTS.md`); also owns one-time legacy migration.
   memory_policy.py             Machine-readable memory/unattended-execution policy: the context→destinations→approval→cap→undo matrix, the `[review]` destination vocabulary, the unattended deferral list, and the capsule's unattended guidance string. Behavior-free; implementation stays in memory_proposals.py/memory_tool.py and the stock assets, which tests pin to this module.
   memory_receipts.py           Recoverable receipts for managed memory mutations. Every region write, queue resolution and prune records a stable-id receipt in the per-workspace `Workspace/Memory-Receipts.jsonl` (append-only JSONL, latest row per id wins) with actor (`operator`/`agent`/`auto`/`system`), surface (`pwa`/`cli`/`archive`/`mcp`/`startup`), expected/before/after revisions, before/after images and a `prepared`→`applied`/`rolled_back`/`failed`/`conflict`/`undone` status. `commit_region_change` serializes the read-merge-write under the guide lock (a lock that cannot be taken is a retryable `MemoryLockError`, never a fall-through to an unlocked write) and re-checks the revision before the atomic replace, so an external direct edit is a `RevisionConflict` rather than a silent overwrite. Every managed proposal-queue writer (`append_proposals`, `remove_proposal_by_substring`, the PWA single/batch/sweep routes) and the queue undo share one re-entrant per-file `queue_lock` (kept outside the vault so the tree stays byte-identical), so the undo's read-check-replace is atomic against a concurrent archive append or CLI dismissal. The PWA routes dispatch the whole locked transaction through `asyncio.to_thread`; a prepared row is written before the rewrite (and a failed preparation aborts the rewrite), so a crash leaves recovery evidence that stays undoable, and settlement completes the decision sidecar before recording the terminal row. `_append` writes the journal, its lock and the trim temp as 0600 (a receipt images whole note bodies, so a new file is never group- or world-readable; an existing one keeps the mode its owner set), and trims the journal while holding the journal lock, bounding it in bytes as well as in rows without ever dropping a non-terminal receipt. `recover_pending` reconciles interrupted receipts from the images at startup, completing a missing decision record idempotently; an uncertain outcome becomes a conflict and is left view-only. Discovery scans each workspace vault journal and each agent root's guide-local fallback journal. `undo_receipt` refuses a changed destination and rejects legacy/unsupported rows. Read side: `GET /api/memory/receipts` and `POST /api/memory/receipts/{id}/undo`. A caller-bound region write takes `authorize` (a factory for a context manager, held through the `prepared` row, the guide replacement and the `applied` row; the lock order is guide, then queue, then state, and `authorize_holder` in `curation_run.py` is the state-lock check); a receipt carrying an `operation_id` is settled by recovery without a decision, and its owning operation finds it with `find_receipts_by_operation_id` and records the decision itself.
    note_receipts.py             Revision-checked note writes: the receipt protocol narrowed to one existing `.md` file inside the canonical configured vault, so an arbitrary note change can be recovered and undone. `commit_note_change` takes a vault-*relative* path — absolute paths, `..`, symlinked files or ancestors, and anything that is not a regular Markdown file are refused, as is the app's own bookkeeping under `Workspace/` (`vault_index.is_reserved_bookkeeping`, the queue and curation logs the memory pipelines maintain themselves); each component is spelled the way the vault has it, so both spellings of a name on a case-insensitive filesystem are one file, one per-file lock and one recorded `relative_path` rather than two writers reaching one note unlocked against each other. Only the vault root is `resolve()`d, so a symlink planted inside the vault cannot redirect the write out of it. **The transaction is whole-note; a range is a composition.** There is no range parameter on `commit_note_change` and there is not going to be one: a receipt keeps a full before image and a full after image precisely so an undo can put a file back without knowing how the file changed. `apply_entry_edit` is a *composer*, not a second protocol — it re-reads the note under the note's own lock, resolves one entry by `note_entries.entry_identity` (which takes no line number and no offset, so a caller whose offsets went stale with the note still gets the entry it named), confirms the entry's `refresh_fingerprint` is still the one that was judged, splices exactly that entry's span (a delete takes the terminating newline with it, so a removed bullet does not become a blank line) and hands the composed `after_text` to `commit_note_change` unchanged. A replacement must be exactly one list item, checked through the same parser the entry came from; a re-stamp is not a separate case but a replacement whose fingerprint is unchanged, which is what `entry_verification.stamp_entry` produces. An identity that names no entry, or a fingerprint that does not match, is a `RevisionConflict` with nothing written — a fingerprint mismatch never overwrites. An *entry* retirement deletes that one span through this helper and comes back through the same whole-note undo; a whole-file retirement stays `vault_review.trash_note`'s, and this module reaches no trash at all. Notes are read and written as bytes, so a BOM, CRLF pairs and an unterminated final line survive verbatim, and the replacement is revision-checked against the exact text the caller read: a stale expectation is a `RevisionConflict`, not an overwrite. Content that is not UTF-8, or whose image exceeds `MAX_IMAGE_CHARS`, is refused before the mutation, because an image the journal cannot hold would leave an `applied` row nobody can undo. Under the same per-file `queue_lock` the apply takes, the row goes down `prepared` first (a journal that cannot record it aborts the write), the bytes are written to a sibling temp file and `os.replace`d with the mode preserved and the revision rechecked immediately before the rename, and a failed write settles `rolled_back` only while the note still matches its before image. `recover_pending` classifies an interrupted `note_apply` by the note's current revision — after→`applied`, before→`rolled_back`, anything else or a missing note→`conflict` — and never rewrites note content; recovery and undo resolve the target against the vault derived from the *journal's own location*, so a forged or moved receipt cannot write outside it. `undo_note_receipt` restores the before image as its own journaled `note_undo` write carrying `undo_of`, and only then marks the original `undone`; an interrupted undo (including the window between the reverse rename and the original's settlement) is finished by one idempotent `settle_open_undo_links` pass, and an undo that loses that final append says the undo *landed* rather than reporting a write error against a note that is already restored. The before image must still hash to the receipt's own `before_revision` or the undo is refused, so a tampered journal row cannot install content it never recorded; a write that changed nothing is recorded `undoable=False`, so History never offers an undo for it. Undo is not offered for undo. Scope is this one mutation protocol only: the business verification/proposal rules that consume it are a later child. Tests: `tests/test_note_receipts.py`, with `tests/test_memory_receipts.py` as read-only regression coverage.
   note_verification.py         One note's verification: the agent's outcome for a single note (`still_valid`/`update`/`retire`/`unverified`), the evidence behind it, and the durable per-note check state that stops the same question being asked twice. `plan_note_verification` decides without writing and is the ONE place the autonomy rule lives, so the curation worklist and the proposal writer cannot drift into stricter or laxer copies of it: a `still_valid` re-stamps frontmatter `updated:` (through `vault_review._stamp_updated`, never a second copy of its regex) and only from complete coverage plus a citation naming the note; an `update` applies the agent's exact replacement only when every evidence row carries a `source_ref`, the `quoted` text seen there and a `supports` clause, and never a replacement that would empty the note; `retire` is never applied here and no delete/trash/archive primitive is imported at all; missing or unavailable evidence is `unverified` with a cooldown, not a failure and not a proposal. `verify_note` is the only mutation path and it goes through `note_receipts.commit_note_change`, so every edit is journaled and undoable; a stale `expected_revision`, or an edit planned against text the note no longer holds, is a `conflict` with the note untouched and no check recorded, so the caller re-reads and judges the text that is actually there. State lives at `<vault>/Workspace/Note-Checks.json` (versioned `{"schema": 1, "notes": {...}, "entries": {...}}` — the second map is `entry_verification.py`'s per-entry checks, and it did NOT bump the schema, because a version a reader does not recognise drops every check in the file and an additive map is not worth a whole vault's cooldowns; `update_check_state` is that document's one writer precisely so the two maps cannot drop each other's rows, and it is also where the one limitation of that choice is stated: a `note_verification` old enough to have written the file is old enough to rewrite it from its `notes` map alone, which would drop every entry check in the vault on its first record — an accepted downgrade cost, because the alternative is the schema bump that costs this same file's note cooldowns to every install), atomic replace under the queue lock, keyed by vault-relative path and never holding an absolute one, in `RESERVED_UNINDEXED_FILES` so the memory system's own paperwork never ranks against the memories it manages). A check describes ONE revision: `should_check` is False only while a check for the same revision is inside its 30-day cooldown or is waiting on a proposal (#726-C's `proposal_id`), and True the moment the note changes. Tests: `tests/test_note_verification.py`, with `tests/test_note_receipts.py` as read-only regression coverage.
   note_create.py               Pure writer behind `ciao note create`: resolves the type (id or alias; enabled and not hidden) against the vault's category registry, picks the category's folder (or a vault-relative `--path` folder), stamps the canonical frontmatter (`type`, `title`, `description`, `tags`, `created`, `updated`, bare ISO dates) and creates the file exclusively, so an existing note is never overwritten and a refusal writes nothing. Tests: `tests/test_note_create.py`.
   note_related.py              Model-free read behind `ciao note related <path>`: the vault notes connected to one note, so the care run compares a stale note with its neighbours instead of judging it in isolation. A note is related when its `related:`/body links point at the target (`links_to`), when it links to the target (`linked_from`), or when its body names the target's title or an alias as a whole word (`mentions`; frontmatter is stripped, names under 3 characters are skipped). Link relations rank before mention-only notes, then by path, capped at 15 with the overflow counted in `truncated`. Each row carries the note's `content_revision` and `due` (`note_verification.should_check`), the two values a `note verify` payload for that note needs, plus `reopenable`: an attended caller (`attended` is the negation of an unattended scheduled turn) may re-verify an `unverified` check for that revision inside its cooldown, unless a proposal is pending (`note_verification.reopens_unverified`). `due` never changes with the caller. Read-only: it records no search hits and touches no FTS database. Tests: `tests/test_note_related.py`.
   note_edit_proposals.py      The typed `note_edit` proposal a verification's `needs_review` result files (#726-C), mirroring the category sidecar (`vocabulary_proposals.py`) because a queue bullet is one line and a whole-note operation with its before/after images and citations is not: the bullet's payload is a sidecar id and the record lives at `<vault>/Workspace/Memory-Note-Edit-Proposals/<id>.json` (versioned `{"schema": 1, "proposal": {...}}`, atomic replace under the per-file queue lock, strict field validation that refuses a record this version cannot read rather than defaulting the operation it would apply). One record per unit of work: `(note, revision)` for a whole-note operation and `(note, revision, entry_identity)` for one of the three `*_entry` operations (`replace_entry`, `restamp_entry`, `retire_entry`) — the id is derived from exactly that, and the empty entry identity leaves a whole-note id byte-identical to the one this derived before entry operations existed, because two bullets of one note at one revision are two different questions and an id shared by both would leave a reviewer deciding about one while the other silently goes unasked. An entry operation's images are still the NOTE's full text both ways (a bounded patch is a different operation with different rules, and what an accept applies is a whole-note `commit_note_change` either way); what it adds is the entry's identity, the fingerprint its verdict was about and the `(start, end)` span — the span is MEASURED from the note at filing time rather than taken from the caller, so the note must still be at `expected_revision` and the entry must still be there with the fingerprint that was read. `entry_replacement` is the exact inverse of that splice, and it is what lets the accept go through `apply_entry_edit` (resolving the entry again, refusing on any mismatch) instead of writing a whole note's text on the strength of a record; the reader re-asserts it, plus `retire_entry` carrying an empty replacement and a living `after`, and `restamp_entry` whose replacement does NOT change the fingerprint (a reviewer clicking re-stamp must never get a rewrite). so re-filing the same verdict about the same text returns the record on file and writes nothing, and the sidecar is written BEFORE the bullet (a bullet with no record is a row whose accept refuses and keeps; a record with no bullet is litter), then the check is recorded carrying that row's `proposal_id` — a `NoteCheck` for a whole-note operation and an `EntryCheck` keyed by the entry's identity for an entry one, through the one `record_pending_check` that knows which map owns which, which is the only thing holding the note off a second proposal (`note_verification._check_settles` suppresses a pending proposal whatever its cooldown says). It never applies anything: the accept lives with the other accept handlers in `ciao/web/proposal_service.py`, where a `replace`/`restamp` goes through `note_receipts.commit_note_change` (revision-checked, journaled, undoable) and a `retire` is an attended click reaching `vault_review.trash_note`, the reversible review trash — `note_verification` itself can still not import a delete primitive. Deliberately NO "refused forever" marker (the category sidecar has one, and a note edit must not): `settle_note_edit` records the decision and the receipt, then clears the check's `proposal_id`, leaving the check's own 30-day cooldown as the honest expiring record — so a note the owner kept or refused is not re-asked this month, and the moment it is edited the check no longer describes it and it is due again. Tests: `tests/test_note_edit_proposals.py`, with `tests/test_note_verification.py` and `tests/test_web_proposals.py` as end-to-end coverage.
   fact_candidates.py          The provenance record a bounded-region write is stamped with. `candidate_from_proposal` lifts an already-parsed `MemoryProposal` and `memory_proposals._provenance_row` turns it into the row `memory_receipts` commits beside the region change, so a fact saved today can be read back years later: its destination, the transcript message ids it cited, its `[as-of:]`/`[expires:]` bounds, and the schema/extraction/policy versions it was written under. A bullet with no `[idx=N]` yields explicit unknown provenance rather than an invented turn id — a receipt has to be able to say "this archive never said where this came from", which is a different fact from "it came from turn 3". Since #1038 (C7) it also carries `source_anchors: tuple[str, ...]` for the *other* kind of citation: `source_message_ids` is `tuple[int, ...]` (Ciaobot transcript indices, parsed with `int()`) and an imported conversation's anchor is a provider string, so the `provider:session_id:anchor` tag rides in its own field — read off the bullet's `source_section` with `assert_external_provenance` rather than a colon count — while the integer field stays empty and `provenance` stays `"unknown"`. The anchors carry the attribution; the verdict is never upgraded to agree with them, and `attended` stays `None` because an imported conversation has no Ciaobot turn to judge. Additive: a row filed before the field existed still reads back with its anchor. The evidence policy that used to check a candidate against the normalized transcript (`validate_candidate` and the `evidence-policy/v1` verdicts) guarded the archive-time auto-apply and was removed with it in #627; `POLICY_VERSION` stays on the record because receipts written before that still carry it.
  import_decouple.py           Which provider sessions are Ciaobot's own, and which imported facts may cite one (#994, child of #975). A leaf contract module, pure except for the one reader: `ciaobot_own_session_ids(config, workspace)` returns the `(provider, session_id)` pairs Ciaobot owns, read from the two records the engine itself keeps — the chat registry at `<state_path parent>/web_projects.json` (every row whatever its lifecycle: live, archived, helper, plus each row's `previous_session_ids` rotation lineage) and the state store at `state_path` (the copy that outlives a deleted chat row, and which records no provider, so an unattributable id is excluded against every supported provider rather than guessed at). Archived and deleted-but-recorded rows stay excluded because reclaim is **fail-open** (`_reclaim_provider_sessions_async` logs and continues) and the idle sweep only disconnects a provider — it deletes no file, so "archived" never means "gone from disk". `classify_session` answers `ciaobot_own` / `external` / `ambiguous` for a discovered session: a recorded id, a Ciaobot chat id, or the `[CIAO_CONTEXT_BEGIN]` capsule in the session's own first user turn means Ciaobot's own, nothing readable means `ambiguous` (reported, never guessed, never imported), otherwise `external`. `assert_external_provenance` refuses a `provider:session_id:anchor` tag naming a Ciaobot chat id or a recorded Ciaobot-own session, so a Ciaobot-own session is never a fact source. `MIXED_SESSION_POLICY` is `exclude_whole_session`: a session Ciaobot has also used holds prompt scaffolding, injected context, memory-pass output and compaction summaries, and the only "was this turn the user's" signal lives in Ciaobot's registry, not in the file. Reads no operator history and starts no engine or model turn; see `docs/CONVERSATION_IMPORT_FEASIBILITY.md`.
  import_discover.py            Engine-host, opt-in discovery and consent-scoped selection for imported conversations (#1029, C5 child of #975). `discover_sources(config, workspace, sources=…)` lists what the workspace's *configured* sources hold and nothing else: Claude Code through `agent_paths.claude_projects_dir(agent_root_for(config, workspace))` (one slug directory, never a glob over `~/.claude/projects`, so another workspace's sessions cannot appear) and OpenCode through `discover_opencode_sessions` on that same agent root, which needs a reachable server and answers one unpaged `--max-count` page. Listing is delegated to the C3 adapters rather than reimplemented; this module decides what may be offered. It reads **no conversation content** — a candidate is only `lstat`-ed for its size, and a link, a non-regular file or a file over `MAX_SESSION_BYTES` is refused rather than followed — so the refs it returns are names, hints and paths. **Nothing in `available` is claimed `external`**: a session is external only once its own opening turn has been read and carries no Ciaobot marker, and metadata cannot establish that, so every offered row is *undecided* and the direction a caller that decided too early fails in is the refusal. What metadata does decide is Ciaobot's own usage: `classify_for_import` (a thin wrapper over `import_decouple.classify_session` whose `known_ids` is always `ciaobot_own_session_ids(config, workspace)`) marks a recorded id or a `chat-<8 hex>` id `ciaobot_own`, and those rows are excluded **before the file is opened**, so Ciaobot's own sessions are never read at all. A corrupt registry raises rather than degrading to an empty exclusion set. `DiscoveryResult` carries `available`, `excluded` (an `ExcludedSource` per refused row, with a closed reason vocabulary: `ciaobot_own` / `unreadable` / `over_cap`), `unsupported` (a source that could not be listed at all — an OpenCode below the V2 floor is a row saying so, not an empty list that looks like "you have none") and per-provider `truncated`, so a listing that hit `MAX_SESSIONS_PER_SOURCE` or the CLI's own `DISCOVERY_MAX_COUNT` is a page rather than a claim of completeness. `preview_selected` is the other half of the consent boundary and the first function here that opens a conversation: it reads **only** the refs a person selected, rebuilds each path from the configured root rather than from anything a caller supplied (a `source_id` that is not a plain file name is refused), classifies again with the turn it read, and reports anything that is not `external` as an excluded row with its reason. It returns counts, the source's own first date when it has one, the reader's omission record, the effective provider/model (`default_provider_for_workspace` / `default_model_for_workspace`, so the screen cannot promise one provider and run another), an input-volume estimate and `BATCH_CAP` — never a character of a conversation. No extraction and no batch write (C7, C6), no model call, no writes. Tests: `tests/test_import_discover.py`, `tests/test_import_routes.py`; see `docs/CONVERSATION_IMPORT_FEASIBILITY.md`.
  import_extract.py              Tool-less extraction of an imported conversation into review proposals, and nothing else (#1012, child of #975). `extract_facts` takes one already-normalized `NormalizedSession`, runs ONE `providers.oneshot.run_oneshot` turn over its text, and writes every accepted row through `memory_proposals.append_proposals` — the same surface `ciao memory-proposal-add` uses. There is no region, note, entity or learnings write in this module, no agent token and no chat: the boundary is "no tools + backend-only proposal writes", not the prompt, and the system prompt's untrusted-transcript instruction is defense in depth for output quality rather than the thing that makes the claim true. The model therefore cannot edit a note, promote a region, call `ciao`, reach MCP or run a command — the capability is absent, not denied — so an injection inside an imported conversation can cause junk proposals and nothing else. Admission is backend code, not wording: a row's `source_anchor` is resolved against the session's own messages (an unresolved anchor is an invented citation), the `provider:session_id:anchor` tag is built from that message and checked with `import_decouple.assert_external_provenance`, the destination must be in `memory_proposals.DESTINATIONS`, and a date the model wrote into the fact text is dropped rather than believed — the date on an imported fact is the source message's own, appended as the `[as-of: …]` tag the accept path already parses, so import time is never passed off as verification time. A `ciaobot_own` or `ambiguous` session is refused by `import_decouple.classify_session` before any turn runs. `citations` stays empty because both it and `FactCandidate.source_message_ids` are `tuple[int, ...]` (transcript indices): the string anchor rides in `source_section`, which the accept path records as the receipt provenance's `section`, and C7 owns the structured field. Unreadable rows degrade to `ExtractionResult.skipped`, never to an exception that loses the batch; `run_oneshot` returns no token counts, so `usage` records only what is knowable from here. Judgment (is this already in a note, does this supersede it, is this person new) is still open and belongs to a stated fact-augmentation follow-up; see `docs/CONVERSATION_IMPORT_FEASIBILITY.md`.
  import_run.py                The per-batch runner that drives one filed import batch into the ordinary review queue (#1038, C7 child of #975) — and the only new module that child adds. `run_import_batch(config, batch_id, model=…, provider=…)` moves a C6 batch `queued → running → done | failed | partial` and walks its selection one source at a time: resolve the id to a `NormalizedSession` through the C5 adapters (`_resolve_claude_code_ref` rebuilds the file from the workspace's own slug directory; `read_claude_code_session` / `read_opencode_session` read it, and an OpenCode id must be one that workspace's own listing named, because the CLI resolves ids across projects), run C4's one tool-less `extract_facts` turn, then record `record_source` (with the SHA-256 of the normalized session as the content digest), `record_progress` and one `FactProvenance` per bullet this run actually filed — read off the queue by diffing it before and after, because `append_proposals` drops already-queued and already-decided rows silently. **The only durable write in this child is C4's `append_proposals`**: no region, doc, entity or learning write, no control plane, no chat, no model call of its own, and a person still presses accept through the existing `accept_region_fact` path with the *source* message's `[as-of:]` date. C4 is called exactly as its docstring requires — `known_own_ids=ciaobot_own_session_ids(config, workspace)` plus the chat ids derived from it — so `classify_session` refuses a Ciaobot-own or undecided session before any turn runs, and `refused_anchor` is told apart from a single refused *row* by the usage record's `messages_in_prompt == 0`. The model and provider are resolved the way the post-archive memory pass resolves its own call (`resolve_insights_model` keyed on the source's canonical provider), per source, and never from a request body: a client choosing what reads a user's history is not a capability this child has. Cancellation is checked between sources and a cancel mid-step is a conflict rather than a failure — future extraction stops, already-filed proposals stay filed, and the cancelled batch is never finished. A turn that came back unreadable — a failing provider, a timeout, a reply that is not a JSON array — is a source that was *not* read, not an extraction: it is recorded `skipped` with an **empty** digest and counted in `unread`, so the batch settles `partial`/`failed` and the digest key can never claim content that was read when it was not (a genuine "nothing worth filing" has C4's `skipped == 0` and stays an extraction). Any exception out of the run — a corrupt own-session registry, a non-conflict store error, a cancel on shutdown — settles the batch `failed` with a fixed sentence before it propagates, because a stranded `running` batch is the one failure with no way out but a person: `create` refuses a second open batch for the workspace and every later press of run is answered with the batch that is "already running". Every blocking call is on a thread (`asyncio.to_thread`): the coroutine runs on the server's event loop and the OpenCode membership check alone shells out to the CLI under 60s timeouts, so a synchronous call would freeze HTTP and WebSockets for the whole server. Dedupe is C4's exact-text one plus C6's batch key: an unchanged re-run appends nothing, and a changed conversation is a new attempt beside the old bullets, never a silent overwrite.
  import_store.py              Private batch store for conversation-import runs (#1032, C6 child of #975). One batch is one import run over a selection: the selection, per-source content digests, progress (`queued → running → done | failed | cancelled | partial`), cancellation, and per-fact `provider:source_id:anchor` provenance. Filed at `<runtime>/import/import-batches.json`, owner-private (`mkstemp_private`, `make_private_dir`), atomically replaced, mutated under `keyed_lock` plus an owner-private advisory sibling lock, and failing closed on corruption (never silently reset). Creation refuses a Ciaobot-own session against `import_decouple` and re-selecting a conversation a live batch already covers under the `(provider, source_id, content_digest, destination, extraction_revision)` key; one batch at a time per workspace. Cancellation keeps recorded progress and provenance; `prune_expired` drops terminal batches past `IMPORT_SNAPSHOT_RETENTION_DAYS` (30) while retaining accepted fact evidence; `forget` drops only the batch record, never queued proposals or accepted memories. No model call, no extraction — C7 consumes this; see `docs/CONVERSATION_IMPORT_FEASIBILITY.md`.
  import_sources/              The provider-neutral import contract and the source adapters that fill it (#1003, child of #975). `contract.py`: frozen `SourceRef` / `NormalizedMessage` / `NormalizedSession` / `Omission`, each with `to_json`/`from_json` for a stored batch; closed vocabularies (providers `claude_code` and `opencode`, with `claude_account` known and deliberately unsupported; roles; omission kinds) so a consumer can be written against them; `MAX_SESSION_BYTES` as the bound on one read; and no timestamp is ever fabricated — `None` is the honest value for a source with no per-message date, and a file mtime is not one. Every entry a reader does not turn into a message is counted by reason in `omissions`, so a batch can state what it dropped. `claude_code.py`: `read_claude_code_session` reconstructs one main chain from a `~/.claude/projects/<slug>/<sid>.jsonl` transcript the way `transcripts.get_session_messages_full` does (the entries no other entry parents, then up through `parentUuid` **or** `logicalParentUuid`, latest-indexed non-`isSidechain`/`teamName`/`isMeta` leaf), anchors each message on the entry `uuid`, records sidechain/team/meta/non-message/off-chain/non-text/unreadable entries as omissions, stops at the last whole line when a file is over the cap (`truncated`), and refuses a symlink or any non-regular file through `os_support.open_fd(follow_symlinks=False)` without writing to the source. `discover_claude_code_sessions` lists a slug directory as metadata only. No model, no network, no engine; see `docs/CONVERSATION_IMPORT_FEASIBILITY.md`.
   curation_run.py              Deterministic worklist, run budget and lease for the nightly Workspace care run. Each successful acquisition appends a UUID to its descriptive holder label (or default host/PID); callers must use returned `lease.holder` as that acquisition identity. Existing live lease strings remain accepted without validation or schema migration. `end_run` checks the supplied live holder under the state lock before mutation, and progress records keys plus lease renewal in one state replacement; read-only end planning stays outside the lock and ownership is checked again after the scan. This fences expired, missing and replaced holders at the commit seam, including `curation-begin`'s category publication: the scan runs outside the locks, and publication locks the proposal queue then the state lock, re-checks the holder before the sidecar, before its replacement and before the queue append. Legacy mixed-version writers and holderless internal calls are not fenced. The weekly marker and state JSON remain separate files and their joint update is not crash-atomic. `build_worklist_views` preserves the sorted, collected discovery tuple separately from completed-key-filtered remaining work; discovery is still incomplete and is not authoritative pruning evidence. `build_worklist` remains the established remaining-only entry point, and CLI planning budgets and renders remaining work while retaining both views internally. Legacy pruning is unchanged. `build_worklist` answers in code the empty/no-op and due-date questions the Workspace care schedule prompt used to spend model turns on (pending routable proposals, region usage at or over the ~85% consolidation threshold or holding expired entries, aging and event-shaped region entries, stale vault notes, learnings at x3 or x1-past-30-days, the `last_full_pass` marker's age, a weekly guide-body review, a weekly `PASS_RULES` that plans `Memory-Review-Rules.md` / `Task-Rules.md` only when each has at least 5 operator decisions the note was not written from, logs over 64KB, skill proposals), so an idle workspace is computable as idle without a model turn. `PASS_STALE_NOTE` sits directly after `PASS_AUDIT` because both are "verify, don't guess" work over facts somebody has been asserting, and it is not a weekly-hygiene key: a note goes stale on its own clock, not the marker's. It is the **cheap, model-free selection** half of note verification — one `WorklistItem` per stale note, keyed by its vault-relative path, oldest first — and it calls `memory_audit.find_stale_notes` rather than forking the thresholds, exempt types, alias resolution and frontmatter-then-mtime date rule, so the nightly plan, `os-audit`, the Memory Map's `stale` flag and the review queue's `unverified` signal cannot disagree about which notes count. It adds the two filters the audit does not apply: `vault_review.never_queued` (a note the review queue would never show a person — `Workspace/` bookkeeping, templates, completed projects — is not tonight's question, and disuse alone never justifies dropping a durable fact) and `note_verification.should_check` (a note whose check is still in its 30-day cooldown or still waiting on the proposal that check pinned has already been asked; without this the same cooled-down notes take the first slots nightly and the backlog starves). It plans at most `STALE_NOTE_MAX_ITEMS` of them per run, capped AFTER the cooldown filter, because the pass sits ahead of the two required hygiene keys and the budget is a whole-run allowance — an uncapped backlog would spend it and `last_full_pass` could never advance. It reaches no verdict; the one thing it reads is the flagged note's own bytes, to state the `content_revision` its item's `reason` carries as the `expected_revision` the operation requires. `PASS_STALE_ENTRY` sits directly after it, for the same "verify, don't guess" reason and because a note is not the unit a person keeps current: a note's `updated:` is one date for every fact in it, so re-verifying the address last week silently re-certified the landlord's name from 2019 with it. Its selection walks **every** note rather than only the stale ones, and ages each entry on **its own** date where it has one — a valid `[verified:]` stamp is aged from that day against the same review horizon the audit measured the note with, and an unstamped or unusable-stamp entry inherits the note's date, so a bullet stamped two years ago in a note re-stamped yesterday is still work and a whole-note selection can never see it. Exempt types and `never_queued` are the audit's and the queue's, not second copies, and the two stale passes share one `scan_vault` walk. Each item is keyed by `note_entries.entry_identity` — not a path, not a line, not an offset — and filtered by `entry_verification.should_check_entry`, the same predicate `verify_entry` short-circuits on, so the plan cannot offer an entry the operation would refuse to judge. It plans at most `STALE_ENTRY_MAX_ITEMS` per run, a cap separate from the note pass's (a shared allowance would let one enormous fact list spend the whole entry budget on a single note), applied after the filter; order is age descending, then path, then the entry's own position, so two runs produce the same list and a short budget drops the youngest fact. Its `reason` carries the entry's own fingerprint and span, the two values a caller cannot rederive without reimplementing a hash and guessing wrong. A re-stamped fact's fingerprint is unchanged, so a settled entry stops being work immediately. That is the managed `verify_note` operation's work (`control_plane.verify_note` → `note_verification.verify_note`, agent surface `verify_note`, CLI `ciao note verify --payload-file FILE`), which takes the verdict as a payload FILE inside the caller's own workspace root, refuses a payload naming another workspace, runs as one bounded coalesced off-loop read (`async_reads.run_read`, keyed by runtime dir + workspace + note + expected revision + a digest of the whole payload, so two callers only share a read when they ask the same question), journals `source` from the turn (`curation` unattended, `chat` attended), and reports an oversized payload or note as `unverified` rather than `applied`. A `needs_review` verdict there files **exactly one** typed `note_edit` proposal and pins the check to its queue row id — the wiring `note_edit_proposals.py` (#726-C) was written for and had no caller for; auto-applied verdicts file nothing, and a retirement is never applied at all. Before this pass, the Workspace care prompt told the *agent* to open stale notes and stamp `updated:` by hand, which produced no receipt, no check state and no record of who decided. `plan_run` applies the run budget, splitting a pass rather than deferring it whole, and the persisted cursor in `<vault>/Workspace/Curation-State.json` lets a budget-limited run resume at the remainder instead of restarting at pass 1. `begin_run` acquires a TTL lease under a short state lock, and progress/finalization commits validate the exact holder at their locked write seam. This coordinates live acquisitions but does not guarantee that whole curation runs cannot overlap: the planning scan can run after its lease expires while another run acquires the vault, which is why publication re-checks the holder under the locks. A category sidecar written while authorized can remain after a later loss, with no queue bullet; the sidecar is written before its bullet by design, and a live retry rewrites that id and appends the bullet once. The bounded `curation-begin` check after planning still rejects an expired or replaced holder. The CLI returns exit 75 for a busy or lost lease, including when the supplied holder is expired, absent from the lease record, or replaced; omitting the required `--holder` argument is a separate usage error. Nothing else holds this lease: the archive-time auto-apply that used to stand down while it was held was deleted with the one-shot pipeline (#627), so the Workspace care schedule prompt's own consolidation is the only writer that lease serializes now. `end_run(advance_marker=True)` stamps `last_full_pass` only once both required weekly checks are recorded done and only after it has confirmed the caller still owns the lease, so an unreliable index refresh or audit — or a run whose lease expired under it — leaves the weekly pass due. CLI: `ciao curation-plan|curation-begin|curation-progress|curation-end`; `curation-progress` and `curation-end` require the `--holder` `curation-begin` returned, and exit 75 means the lease is busy or the supplied holder lost ownership (expired, missing from the lease record, or replaced).
   curation_logs.py             Bounded byte primitives for the packaged decision logs (`Workspace/Curation-Log.md`, `Weekly-Review-Log.md`): no-follow bounded reads, the exact bytes a managed append or the weekly `last_full_pass` marker produces, and private atomic publication that keeps an existing file's owner, group and mode. It never takes the curation state lock or reads the lease; `curation_run.append_log` and `_write_full_pass_marker` hold that lock around every call.
   curation_evidence.py         Read-only, revision-bound evidence for one due stale note (#3052). `prepare_one_stale_note` takes the first planned stale-note item naming exactly one note, reads it through a no-follow regular-file descriptor (64 KiB ceiling) and compares its revision with the plan, then lists the open and settled note-edit proposals that name the same note from the sidecar store and the live queue. Any doubt (wrong vault, changed or unsafe note, malformed or over-limit store, more than 20 references) returns a `DeferredNote` with a fixed reason; nothing is written.
   curation_judgments.py        One read-only model judgment of one prepared stale note (#3237). `judge_prepared_note` makes at most one provider attempt (`run_oneshot`, no retries, timeout finite and clamped to 120 s; a non-finite or non-positive timeout raises `ValueError`) and only for a `PreparedNote`; `NothingToJudge` makes no call. The evidence goes to the model as one JSON line, every string capped at 200 characters. `parse_judgment` validates the reply (16 KiB ceiling, duplicate-key rejection, exact envelope and echo, reference membership, current-revision live proposal for `attach`, no lone surrogates) into a closed `Validated`/`Invalid`/`TimedOut`/`Unavailable` outcome with a fixed-reason attempt record; it never raises out of the judge. `verified` is always refused in production (no independent evidence input). `section` and `change` are unverified model text: a consumer must validate them against the live note before acting, and must re-run preparation and compare `revision` first. Nothing is written.
   skill_catalog.py             Bounded, read-only skill catalog candidates for a care run (#3248). `prepare_skill_candidates` lists installed skills that have a reason to look (an archived use or an open or settled proposal or upstream draft), never age or installation alone, and routes each by ownership: workspace-owned to the skill-proposal queue, packaged to the upstream-draft queue. Exclusions (paused or disabled schedule, unreadable or changed source) come before the `MAX_CANDIDATES_PER_RUN` cap, so a protected skill never takes a slot. `judge_candidates` hands each selected candidate to `curation_judgments.judge_prepared_note` as a `PreparedNote`. Candidates are interleaved across workspace and packaged skills before the cap; with no persisted cursor the same skills repeat nightly. Nothing is written, and no schedule calls it yet.
   care_cache_key.py            Dormant, pure cache-key model for one model judgment of a stale note (#3057). `judgment_key` keys the FRESH `PreparedNote`, so preparation always re-runs and deferred, unselected or unreadable outcomes are `Uncacheable`; the key covers every field of the note and its references, the exact model-visible prompt with neutral attempt ids, the constants the preparer and validator read, the workspace, and the resolved provider and model. An incomplete input raises `IncompleteKey`, never a partial key. `EXCLUDED_FROM_KEY` names what must stay out (run and batch ids, timeout, planner due-time constants). `check_parity` checks recorded full-path outcomes for incomplete keys, stale hits, cached uncertain output and declared changes that left the key unchanged. Nothing calls it yet and nothing is stored.
   curation_cycle.py            Pure, write-free codec for the care cycle, schema v2 (#3061 phase A). `encode_state`/`decode_state` are strict (exact fields, refused duplicates, canonical dates, `bool` is not an int, v1 documents refused); `project_obligations` counts only the cycle's own evidence, so old global hygiene keys never certify a new cycle; `plan_migration` is a dry run that creates no files; `decide_recovery` maps the marker bytes found after an interrupted publication to resume, commit or block. `curation_run` imports it for two things only: the v2 facade that lets the existing consumers read a cycle file, and the operator-run `curation-cycle-migrate` (#3061 B1). `migration_report` previews and `migrate_state_to_cycle` applies the v1 to v2 move under the state lock, after copying the exact v1 bytes to `Workspace/Curation-State.v1.bak`; on a cycle state, `end_run(advance_marker=True)` prepares the weekly marker transition in the state before it publishes the marker, then rotates the cycle and releases the lease in one store (#3061 B2). Every other entry point settles an interrupted transition from the marker bytes before it writes: the prior bytes are published once more, the target bytes are committed, anything else blocks for an operator, and a live lease is never taken over. Nothing runs the migration on a schedule, and it does not change `curation_run.STATE_VERSION`.
   curation_apply.py            The one managed accept for an unattended `[memory]`/`[profile]` queue row (#3053). `plan_memory_accept` binds a row to the proposal, prediction and destination revisions the caller judged; `apply_memory_accept` re-checks the per-kind acceptance policy (#3242), the live lease held by the caller's holder, the cap and the row under the guide, queue and state locks (in that order) before the guide write and again before the queue settlement, then records one `via="agent"` decision carrying its `operation_id`, linked to both receipts. A crashed operation is finished from `find_receipts_by_operation_id`; a retry whose queue row is already gone finds its unfinished queue receipt and resumes it under the same handover, lease and cap checks (`resume_memory_accept`); a row the guide already settled but the queue no longer matches returns `PartialConflict` and is left to recovery and undo. A resume after the region write refuses once the operator's per-kind policy forbids the kind, and does not finish the decision or progress key: the fact stays in the guide and is undoable from History. `ciao memory-proposal-accept` is a thin caller.
   vault_review.py              Deterministic workspace-scoped vault-note review candidates, append-only dispositions, reversible trash/restore, project completion, and attended permanent deletion; bookkeeping is excluded from ordinary review candidates. A candidate is identified by (workspace, vault-relative path, content hash), so an edited note cannot inherit an old destructive decision. Dispositions: `keep` (stamps `updated:`), `reopen` (undoes a keep), `trash`/`restore` (reversible workspace trash), `delete` (attended, requires a trashed note plus exact confirmation), `complete` (close a project out), `vanished` (written by the system for a note that left the vault some other way). `complete` is the only disposition that MOVES a note rather than removing it: `complete_project_note` takes a note whose declared `type:` is `project`, or any note under a `projects/` segment that declares no type at all (a declared type always wins, so a `type: person` note filed under `projects/` is not a project), and moves it to `projects/completed/` — the whole folder for a `projects/active/<slug>/` project, the single file for a flat `projects/<name>.md` — rewriting `status: active` to `status: completed` using the same `status:` line anchor as the PWA's own completion, matched inside the frontmatter block here and substituted over the whole text there, because a review candidate is any note in the vault and a body line reading `status: active` — inside a fenced example, a pasted transcript — is prose rather than the note's own state. Whatever the candidate was, a folder project moves as a FOLDER: every note under it moves, so every reference to any of them is repointed in both dialects (`related:`/wikilink and relative markdown) via the pure `vault_rehome.rewrite_references` primitive, and a candidate can be a note nested below the entry markdown (`projects/active/<x>/meetings/2026-01.md`), with `new_path` naming where that note lands inside the moved folder. It is one transaction: the links are resolved and rewritten BEFORE the move (resolution needs the notes on disk), every rewrite is staged and swapped as a unit, each file is re-read and compared against the text its rewrite was computed from immediately before the swap, and a failure at any point — staging, the move, or the ledger append — restores every file already written and leaves the project where it was, because a row claiming a closed project while notes still point at the old path is the one state this workflow must not produce. The ledger row records `previous_path`, `new_path`, `status_rewritten`, `edited_backlinks` and the `undo` images that make it reversible, each keyed at the path its note has WHILE COMPLETED, so a note inside a moved folder and a note that referred to it need two different keys. Reads and writes in this feature go through exact-newline helpers, so a CRLF note keeps its line endings and a rollback is byte-exact. `restore_completed` is the reverse: it undoes only the latest `complete` row for an id, and refuses when there is none, when the completed note is gone, when the original path or project folder is occupied (`shutil.move` would nest it), or when a note the completion rewrote has been edited since. A `complete` row is terminal and suppresses: the note is no longer at the path the row names, and a completed project is already exempt from candidates (`never_queued`) and from `vanished` rows, which would otherwise report the move as a deletion. It also reads the managed verification state (#726-E), so the queue and the pass cannot ask the same question twice: `read_note_checks` is consulted once per scan, a check that `_check_settles` (the very predicate `verify_note` short-circuits on and the curation worklist filters its plan by) says already answers the note's current revision clears the `unverified` signal, and a check carrying a `proposal_id` for THAT revision is reported as a **pending verification** rather than as a second decision to make. The candidate payload then carries `pending_verification` (the queue row and the sidecar id behind it, with the outcome, coverage, citations and the reason) and `retirement_offered`, and the panel links the proposal instead of offering *Still true* / *Retire* on the same revision — two buttons in two places asking one question and able to disagree is the defect. `retirement_offered` is False only when a pending proposal is the note's SOLE reason for being queued: another signal (unlinked, duplicate, superseded wording) is an independent finding about the note and the queue must not lose it because the pass reached the same note first. A proposal pinned to a revision the note has since left is reported as `conflicted` and suppresses nothing, because its accept would refuse as a conflict and the note is due again. The same scan reads the entry-level detector (#785) and reports it as a separate `unverified_entries` signal plus an `entry_verification` evidence block: the exact list items with the lines around them, whether the date on one is its own stamp or the file's `updated:` carried onto it, and the pending `note_edit` proposals keyed by the same entry identity. It **links** those proposals rather than offering a second accept — the row's own buttons are about the whole file, and a fact is not the file — and reports `conflicted` on an entry proposal the accept would refuse. `unverified_entries` is in `CHECK_ONLY_SIGNALS`, a set rather than one hard-coded name precisely so a new "check this" signal cannot start offering Retire on a note whose only finding is that nobody read one of its bullets. Coverage (`uncovered`, i.e. prose, a table or a quote nothing here can read as a fact) is reported in the same block and is deliberately **not** a signal: a note whose facts live in prose is unmeasured, not wrong, and making it a row would fill a queue whose terminal action is deletion with every short note in the vault. `checked_at` rides along separately from the note's own `updated:` and the two are never collapsed: a verdict that came back `unverified` writes nothing, so a note can be checked yesterday and still carry last year's `updated:`.
  memory_proposals.py          The memory-proposal queue, the bounded regions it promotes into, and the receipts both leave. A proposal is one bullet in `Workspace/Memory-Proposals.md` tagged with its destination (`memory`/`profile`/`project`/`people:<Name>`/`review`); a bullet may end with a third optional tail `_(decide: nightly|you; reason)_` after the `from` and `request` tails, parsed by `proposal_kinds.parse_bullet` into `decider`/`decide_reason`, and `set_proposal_decider` re-tags one bullet in place under the queue lock for `ciao memory-proposal-escalate`; a person or the agent files one by hand (`ciao memory-proposal-add`, `append_proposals`), the review surface lists and dismisses them, and a user accepts one into its destination (`accept_region_fact`, `reconcile_region_fact`). Regions get a trailing learned-at `[YYYY-MM-DD]` stamp and dedupe compares stamp-stripped. `reconcile_region_fact` is the ADD/UPDATE/COVERED step on the accept path: one small model call compares the new fact against current entries so a superseded entry is replaced (old text to the Memory-Consolidations undo log first) instead of accumulating beside its successor; any failure degrades to the plain append. The write is not a plain append: it goes through the `ciao.memory_audit` event-shape guard, stamps the `fact_candidates` provenance row, and logs every replace. A new lesson goes directly through `ciao memory learn` and `append_learning`, with recurrence evidence stored by `learning_records`. The entity-note writer is the category's, not a hardcoded folder's: `write_entity_note`/`entity_note_path` take the notes root the note is written under AND the agent vault root the category is read from (`entity_types.load_entity_types`; the two are the same directory only on a re-rooted install) and refuse a category that has no folder, is disabled, is unknown, or whose folder is not one inside the notes root, so a `[people]` accept lands in `People/<Name>.md` typed `person`, a category the owner added has a writer too, and an owner's `person.folder: Humans` is what an accept honours (#637); `render_entity_note` is the same body without the write, so the review card's preview and the accept cannot disagree. `record_dismissal`/`record_promotion` write every decision (web, CLI and accept alike) to the per-workspace `Memory-Proposals.dismissed.jsonl` sidecar with `via` (pwa/agent), `destination` and `outcome`; Each sidecar row carries an optional single-line `reason` (validated at the edges with `task_decisions.clean_reason`, flattened by `_one_line` because the sidecar splits on `splitlines`); the receipts journal is trimmed and is therefore NOT where reasons live, and `read_decisions` is the enumerator the weekly pass reads. `read_decisions`/`history_row_id` are its read side, served by `GET /api/proposals/history` — what was accepted or dismissed, by whom, and from where. The one-shot producer of these bullets is gone (#627): the memory pass, a chat of the app's own, writes memory directly now.
  learning_records.py           The canonical record model for `Workspace/Learnings.md` (#732, #755): `parse_learnings` reads a learnings document into `LearningRecord`s with immutable, workspace-scoped identities, `render_learning` writes the canonical line plus a machine-readable `<!-- ciao:learning {...} -->` comment, `migrate_learnings` rewrites only recognized Active entry spans while preserving every other byte, and `observe_learning` folds one sighting into a record. Its reason to exist is that recurrence was counted from a retry-sensitive prose comparison and a four-word key; identity here is persisted, not derived, so rewording a statement keeps it. Unknown stays unknown (a legacy bullet renders `unknown` / `?` rather than an invented today and x1), a malformed date or a broken comment is diagnosed with its bytes untouched rather than guessed at, observations dedupe on source/turn/request and never on excerpt wording, and a source-less sighting is refused because nothing can tell it apart from a new one. Display citations cap at eight; the comment carries every observation. Since #755 this is the *only* reading and writing of a learnings line: the private regex, key, normalizer and line formatter in `memory_proposals.py` were deleted rather than shadowed, `memory_proposals.append_learning` and `curation_run._learning_items` are two callers of this model, and `LEARNINGS_RELATIVE`/`LEARNINGS_STUB`/`SECTION_HEADINGS` live here so the writer, the reader and the migration cannot disagree about where the file is or what an empty one says. `normalized_statement` is the one place a first sighting is matched against prose — only possible while an entry still carries no comment — and `allocate_learning_id` mints deterministically so callers computing the same sighting land on identical bytes.
  learnings_migrate.py          The one-off conversion of an installed `Workspace/Learnings.md` onto that model. Dry-run by default, `--apply` to write, `--revert <receipt>` to put the original bytes back. Only the spans of recognized `## Active` entries are replaced: frontmatter, format notes, the whole `## Promoted / Resolved` section, a BOM and CRLF line endings pass through byte for byte, and an entry the parser could not read is reported, kept exactly, and reflected in a non-zero exit — a shape nothing downstream can count must not be reported as a finished run. The migrated text and the recorded spans come out of one pass, so the receipt is an exact inverse of the bytes written rather than a re-derivation of what the line probably said. Receipts are one timestamped `learnings-<YYYYMMDD-HHMMSS>.json` per applied run under `<runtime>/migration/`, never overwritten; each span is re-checked before it is replaced and a file with one mismatch is left entirely alone. The write takes the same per-file lock direct learning writes take, and goes through the same atomic sibling-temp-plus-rename shape, in bytes rather than text so a CRLF file is not rewritten by newline translation. `Learnings.md` is bookkeeping, not an entity note, so it is deliberately not a `commit_note_change`. Idempotent because a migrated line renders as itself: there is no state to invalidate, because the content says whether it has been done. CLI: `ciao learnings-migrate`.
  learnings_queue_drain.py   One write-ahead batch receipt drains all legacy `[learnings]` rows. It stores validated before/after images for Learnings.md and Memory-Proposals.md, binds the registered workspace and canonical vault, and is private under `<runtime>/migration/learnings-queue-drain/`. Apply/recovery holds the queue then learnings lock through planning and writes; previews only inspect. History stays append-only through stable operation IDs, with recovery repairing an exact partial final row. Revert verifies both destination revisions, restores exact prior bytes/existence, and records reversal events.
  vocabulary_proposals.py      Vault vocabulary promotion/merge proposals: a non-canonical type or singleton tag that crosses the usage threshold (5 shared with vault_index) becomes a promotion candidate for CANONICAL_TYPES, and a singleton near-duplicate tag (case-only variant, a value under an established bare parent, or length-scaled edit distance) becomes a merge candidate for aliasing — distinct values in a namespace only merge when the bare stem is itself established at or above the threshold, repeated case-only variants are folded into the dominant spelling (which is the only spelling promoted), and reciprocal singleton-singleton aliases are collapsed to one direction. Counts aggregate across every workspace vault via config.vault_scan_targets() (preserving an empty shared-vault stamp) and report skipped roots as scan errors. Informational only — surfaced inline in the workspace hygiene audit (ciao/os_audit.py, section 8) without raising the audit status, mirroring the Memory-Proposals.md promote/dismiss pattern.
  recurrence.py                Pure recurrence verdict for a repeated procedure (#3238-A): `classify_recurrence` counts independent outcomes from caller-resolved completions, folding exact repeats and same-task completions, and reports `unavailable` when no claim resolved so a failed lookup never reads as `below`. No I/O, no locks; `RECURRENCE_THRESHOLD` is 3.
  recurrence_evidence.py       Read-only lookup for the recurrence gate (#3238-B): `resolve_completions` maps the completion ids a new-skill draft cites to the task file that persisted each one, through the task board's own reader. It takes no lock and writes nothing, bounds the ids it resolves (16) and the task-named files it counts (2000), and ignores non-task files. An unreadable `<32hex>.md` task is reported on `Lookup.unreadable`, and the gate turns a not-met verdict into `unavailable` only when a cited id is unresolved, so a truncated file cannot be read as absence. Known limit: `Workspace/Learnings.md` is model-written, so the gate proves the cited completions exist and are distinct tasks, not that they are instances of one procedure.
  observations.py              Counted observations (#1338): `Workspace/Observations.md` is a second learnings-shaped file (`search: false`, parsed and rendered by `learning_records`) holding weak behavioural patterns the memory pass records with `ciao observation-add`. The subject is a fixed `About the user:` / `About <Name>:` prefix of the statement, and each chat adds at most one sighting. At three distinct chats the Workspace care run's `observations` pass runs `ciao observation-propose`, which queues an ordinary `[profile]` or `[people <Name>]` proposal and moves the entry to `## Promoted / Resolved`, where it is never proposed again; with `--drop` it is moved there without a proposal, for an entry the care run judges would not change agent behaviour; `observation-merge` unions two entries about the same subject and `observation-prune` archives x1 entries older than 60 days to `Observations-Archive.md`. Nothing here writes a region or a person note. `classify_weak_sightings` (#3245) is a pure, I/O-free contract for new weak evidence: each `(source_id, candidate_id)` sighting expires independently at seven days of its own UTC capture, with conflicting, future, naive or identity-less sightings reported as unavailable.
  learnings_cleanup.py         The settlement-linked reconciliation of `Workspace/Learnings.md` (#728-E): `plan_cleanup` is a pure plan over the document, one `learning_cleanup_eligibility` question per Active entry plus the upstream draft sidecar, and it writes nothing — no file, no store, no receipt. `apply_cleanup` re-reads under the file lock, re-checks the whole-document revision (a concurrent direct learning write lands in a *different* offset range and a per-entry compare is silent about it, so a cleanup that spliced on the plan's offsets would drop the new lesson with the old one), re-asks every candidate's eligibility against the bytes under that lock, splices the eligible Active spans out back-to-front, and writes through the migration's `_write_locked(expect=…)`. Every other byte survives — frontmatter, format notes, fenced examples, the whole `## Promoted / Resolved` section, a BOM, CRLF — with exactly one exception: the frontmatter's `updated:` is restated (added if absent), because a document that still dates itself to the day its last entry was retired misreports its own age to every consumer that reads that field. The reverse map is built and serialized *and persisted* before the file is written, per span, with the exact bytes removed, the offset the gap occupies in the text the run leaves (which for the second and later removals of a batch is further left than the span's own start, by the bytes every lower span took — two entries removed side by side share one offset and one gap), and the context either side of it, sampled from the text that is actually written (a removal leaves nothing at the offset to check, so an undo verifies the context instead); `unmigrate_cleanup` fills the gaps from those bytes and refuses the whole file on one mismatch. A receipt that cannot be persisted removes nothing, and a run that dies between the two writes leaves an inert receipt the undo refuses — while the two failures a run *can* catch take their receipt back, so only a process that actually dies can leave one behind. Idempotence is a store, not a re-render: `Workspace/learnings-cleanup.json` records `(learning_id, entry_revision)` pairs that have been removed, the planner skips a suppressed pair, and an undo deliberately does *not* lift it — otherwise the next nightly pass removes the line the operator just put back. The undo re-records the pairs from the receipt instead, because the apply writes the document first and the store second, and an undo that restored a line nothing holds would hand it straight back to the next pass. A changed entry is a new revision and is eligible again on its own merits; a store that exists and cannot be read blocks the plan entirely rather than reading as empty, because every pair in it is an entry already removed once. "Unchanged since filing" is per entry (`learning_records.entry_revision`, the hash of the canonical line), not per file; already-filed whole-file revisions match nothing and are kept rather than backfilled, since rewriting one to match the present file would assert a sameness nothing established.
